anam explainer

commit c08bfb5 (2026-10-20) · generated 2026-11-01 · 175 concepts · 0 inferred, 0 local · 17 gaps

purpose Terms Components Workflows Data structures Decisions Invariants Change paths Gaps

Anam, a personal longitudinal record

One person's whole-life timeline of facts, assembled verbatim from the systems that already hold their data, owning none of it, with a receipt for everything it shows.

What it does not do

  • It owns no source fact. Every payload is verbatim from its source and rebuildable; delete Anam and no fact is lost. It does own the continuity, the composed order, which is re-derivable but held nowhere else.
  • It never parses a payload into a canonical schema, never joins and never summarises. Weekly averages are projections or derived facts, not admitted facts.
  • It never overwrites or deletes. Corrections supersede; retraction is a tombstone event; the ledger's triggers refuse update and delete for every writer.
  • It does not schedule, poll, or know a vendor's tables. Ductile decides when; an importer knows the vendor; Anam knows Definitions, the gate and the record.
  • It does not retract on its own. An importer reports what its source still holds; the operator retracts, with guards.
  • It does not gate on classification or consent yet. The attribute and the pass-through gate exist; the policy is deferred.
  • It does not serve multiple people, and it is not a dashboard: it answers "what did I know about this on that date, and from where" rather than "what is the number now".

Core concepts

  • Admission gate The only ingress: resolve binds an envelope to the person, commit validates it against its Definition, composes identity, and appends through the ledger idempotently.
  • Fold Derives each facet's current status from its anam-events in seq order and builds the canonical value, sorted and hashed.
  • Ledger store (internal/store/ledger) The append-only SQLite file: facet artifacts, anam-events and ledger_meta, with triggers that refuse update and delete for every writer.
  • Admit a batch One blood-pressure export dropped in a watched folder becomes a facet in the ledger, an index row, and a minted checkpoint, with every call between the importer repo and the service traced.
  • Importers live outside the binary, as ductile plugins A source is a Python plugin in anam-importers that knows one vendor and shapes rows to a Definition; one generic anam_submit posts under a per-source token; Anam keeps the Definitions, the gate and the record and knows no vendor.
  • Anam owns no source fact No captured payload exists only inside Anam; every one is verbatim from its source and rebuildable, so deleting Anam loses no fact. Anam does own the continuity.
  • Current status is derived by the fold, never stored on the artifact A facet carries its birth status and nothing else; admitted, superseded, tombstoned, endorsed and rejected are folded from anam-events on every read.
  • commit is idempotent and atomic Artifact, anam-event and watermark land in one transaction in one file; same identity and same hash is a no-op, a different hash supersedes, and a refused batch writes nothing, so a worker may crash mid-batch and be re-run to known-good.

Components

Primary workflow: Admit a batch

One blood-pressure export dropped in a watched folder becomes a facet in the ledger, an index row, and a minted checkpoint, with every call between the importer repo and the service traced.

Gaps

  • A mint that fails after an importer batch is reported only in the daemon log
  • A reader is not narrowed to projections
  • A weekly bucket width disables composed reads and incremental mints
  • anam_submit's manifest does not declare current_versions
  • config.example.yaml still names the old backup method and a stale store list
  • Definitions are registered on a 404, not at start
  • docs/API.md describes a conversational API that is mostly unbuilt
  • facet_index is written by catch-up but read by no read path
  • Receipts pin the bundled Definition set only
  • Retract runs against the local files, never the daemon, and leaves the index behind
  • Sentences in ARCHITECTURE.md the code has moved past
  • The drill is the only rebuild so far
  • The interpretation source is decided and not built
  • The ledger has one secondary index
  • The sync_cursor table is never written
  • token_scope is a dead table
  • Whether writing an id field counts as composing identity is not settled

Purpose

Anam, a personal longitudinal record One person's whole-life timeline of facts, assembled verbatim from the systems that already hold their data, owning none of it, with a receipt for everything it shows.

purpose basis: docs

Primary workflow Admit a batch

The problem

Data about one person is scattered: a watch measures sleep and heart rate, a scale measures the body, a cuff measures blood pressure, a lab prints a report, and each lives in its own silo. No single place shows the person to themselves over time with the evidence attached, and nothing they would trust to say what it knew, and when.

Anam (Irish for soul) is that place for exactly one person. It reads their sources, holds each fragment verbatim as a Facet with where it came from and when it was true, and lays them on one timeline. The whole-life read is the default; a span is a lens the reader may add. Every fact accretes and nothing is overwritten, so the past stays as recorded, and every view it serves comes with a receipt proving exactly what was shown at what moment of knowledge.

The repo is the Go service and CLI that own the record. Its sources now arrive through a second repo, anam-importers: Python ductile plugins that each know one vendor and submit envelopes over HTTP. This explainer covers both, because the contract between them is where most of the design shows.

The whole of it is drawn in one diagram: sources and importers on the left, the daemon and the files it alone opens in the middle, the readers on the right. Its Excalidraw source, docs/assets/anam-overview.excalidraw, sits beside it.

Who it is for

Matt, as the one person recorded, as the operator who runs the daemon on unraid, and as the author of each importer. Agents acting for him are the second consumer: they read projections, with a reader token since card 89, and may one day propose findings back through the same gate. There is no second person, so there is no identity graph and no merge.

What it does not do

  • It owns no source fact. Every payload is verbatim from its source and rebuildable; delete Anam and no fact is lost. It does own the continuity, the composed order, which is re-derivable but held nowhere else.
  • It never parses a payload into a canonical schema, never joins and never summarises. Weekly averages are projections or derived facts, not admitted facts.
  • It never overwrites or deletes. Corrections supersede; retraction is a tombstone event; the ledger's triggers refuse update and delete for every writer.
  • It does not schedule, poll, or know a vendor's tables. Ductile decides when; an importer knows the vendor; Anam knows Definitions, the gate and the record.
  • It does not retract on its own. An importer reports what its source still holds; the operator retracts, with guards.
  • It does not gate on classification or consent yet. The attribute and the pass-through gate exist; the policy is deferred.
  • It does not serve multiple people, and it is not a dashboard: it answers "what did I know about this on that date, and from where" rather than "what is the number now".

Terms (36)

Anam-event An assertion that Anam's knowledge changed, in one of exactly five kinds, each with a gapless sequence number.

term

An anam-event records that Anam's knowledge changed. There are exactly five kinds: admitted, superseded, tombstoned, endorsed and rejected. Each carries anam-time and a monotonic, gapless seq. Every kind can change the live-set, so the watermark is the sequence itself.

Publication is deliberately not an anam-event: serving a facet changes nothing Anam knows, and a receipt records what was shown. An anam-event references a facet; a facet never references an anam-event. The row is the anam-event row.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Publication is not an anam-event
  • invariants Agent findings are derived facets admitted as proposed; endorsement never writes back, W is the anam-event sequence itself: one number, gapless, never stored
  • mentioned by Anam-time, Facet, Fold, Ledger, Mint, Status, Supersession, Tombstone, Watermark
Anam-time When Anam's knowledge changed; the clock an as-of reads, and an audit instrument rather than a history one.

term

Anam-time is the clock carried by anam-events: when Anam learned something. It orders events, and because seq is the watermark, an anam-time resolves to exactly one watermark. An unqualified --as-of always means anam-time.

A bulk backfill compresses its span: thirty months of fact-time can arrive in one afternoon of anam-time, so an as-of inside a backfill returns whatever prefix had landed by then. That is honest: Anam did not exist during the backfilled period. It makes anam-time an audit instrument, what a receipt stood on, rather than a history instrument.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Two clocks, not four
  • mentioned by Anam-event
Attention (the mind domain) What the person says is absorbing them, kept as a reading with a topic and a strength from 1 to 5, and as a declared start and end; the first concept in the mind domain.

term

mind is the ninth domain head. It holds what a person says about their own inner state, so mood or energy can join later as concepts without another head. A head is added only by a logged decision, and this one was decided on 2026-10-22 (card 222); see where a distinction is encoded. Anam records what the person said and does not judge it. A model's guess at where attention went is a different source and sits beside these facts without editing them.

Attention is the first concept. Three types are built, each a Definition bundled with the binary.

  • mind.attention is one reading. The person says, at a moment, what is holding them (topic), how strongly (intensity, a whole number from 1 to 5) and, if they like, a note. It is a reading in the way a blood pressure is, but the instrument is the person.
  • mind.attention.started and mind.attention.ended are a pair the person declares, for how long something held them. Their series key is the topic, so an ended closes the latest started of that topic and a later started opens a new one. Readings never open or close an episode. Nothing here works episodes out from readings, so a gap between readings means nothing until the person says it.

The topic has one agreed spelling, lower case with words joined by hyphens (deskcam), the way a drug has one spelling. The store never reads inside a payload, so a topic can be found only by matching it exactly, and a second spelling would be a second topic. The Definition's pattern refuses spaces and capitals.

The moment is a wall clock the person said it at, so each type reads at with the offset that source_time carries, as health.medication.dose does. The id is the topic and the minute, then the offset: one minute said in two zones is two readings, and the same minute said twice is one. The classification is Confidential, as for the other things a person does with their time. The bundled attention projection is a log of the three types.

A fourth, mind.attention.reflection, is not built. It would hold something said later about a period, with the person's own verdict. It waits until a question needs it, as do a bounded session and a deliberate shift from one topic to another.

Hand entry: the Workbench draws a form for any type the importers file lists under its workbench principal, so the three types are entered by hand once an operator lists them. An agent saves one through anam-mcp's save_fact.

Where else it is used

  • mentioned by Intake (caffeine, alcohol and notable meals)
Bucket span The minted span, a calendar window over fact-time that every facet belongs to by arithmetic; invisible to the reader.

term

A bucket span is a calendar window over fact-time, one month by default (bucket_width, weekly also implemented), subdivided along the calendar when a bucket's value is over checkpoint_budget. Every facet is a member of exactly one by arithmetic on its fact-time, with no lookup. Bucket ids are self-describing (bucket:2026-07, bucket:2026-W28, a day for a split bucket).

Because facet_id is identity and not contents, every version of a facet carries the same fact-time and lives in one bucket, so the lifelong view is a disjoint union of bucket values. Contrast the derived span, which is never minted.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Bucket on fact-time, monthly by default, subdivided by a byte budget
  • invariants The bucket width is checked once at config load and pinned into the index, Minting is per span, never whole-life
  • mentioned by Checkpoint, Derived span, Fact-time, The whole-person read
Canonical The live-only value of the fold at a watermark; superseded and tombstoned facets are excluded from it.

term

Canonical names exactly one surface: the live-only fold at a watermark, every facet whose current status is live. Superseded and tombstoned facets remain in the ledger, marked, and are absent from the canonical value by construction.

It is what the normal serve path reads and what projections prune. Earlier Anam drafts used the word for the append-everything surface; that is corrected in ARCHITECTURE.md §7.

Where else it is used

  • invariants Canonical means the live-only value; the ledger means append-everything-marked
  • mentioned by Checkpoint, Fold, Ledger, Projection, Tombstone
Checkpoint An immutable, content-addressed value of the live-only fold over one bucket at a watermark, there only to bound replay.

term

A checkpoint is the Canonical value of one bucket span at a watermark, hashed over its bytes and written as a file under that hash. The mint produces it solely to bound read-time replay depth; freshness comes from the ledger, not from minting.

A hard checkpoint has a row in the checkpoint log and a row of checkpoint_value, and a mint removes it once a later checkpoint of its span replaces it as the head (a checkpoint is a cache that may go missing, and a read that cannot find one folds the ledger); a soft one is the same value computed on demand and dropped, never kept between requests. Only a hard one is a replay floor. The stored form is the checkpoint value.

Where else it is used

  • invariants Any state is the nearest checkpoint plus replay since its watermark, Minting is per span, never whole-life
  • mentioned by Fold, Mint, Watermark
Derived span A lens the person names over the timeline, composed on demand from bucket values and never minted.

term

A derived span is an inclusive fact-time interval the person names (--from/--to), optionally narrowed by type, a saved bookmark, or everything. It is composed on demand by stitching bucket values and materialised only when a receipt is served. It is never stored as a unit, never minted, never pulsed.

Conflating it with the bucket span produced the whole-timeline mint that ARCHITECTURE.md §1 retracts: removing the span as a required read filter never required removing it as a mint unit.

Where else it is used

  • decisions The person is the substrate
  • invariants The whole-person read is the default; a span is an optional lens
  • mentioned by Bucket span, Fact-time
Employment (the org domain) Where the person worked and in what role, kept as a start, changes of role and an end, one series per period with an employer; the first concept in the org domain.

term

org is the tenth domain head. It holds the person's relationships with organisations, and employment is the first concept. Membership comes later, as org.membership. A head is added only by a logged decision, and this one was decided on 2026-10-24 (card 239); see where a distinction is encoded. The cost is accepted: org sorts by counterparty where the other heads sort by subject, so a shareholding stays with money.

Three types are built, each a Definition bundled with the binary, written by hand into a records file or saved by an agent.

  • org.employment.started opens a period of employment and carries the role the person started in: a title, and where known the employer's display name, the legal_entity, the location, who the role reported to, the grade and the terms.
  • org.employment.role-changed is a new role inside the same period. It restates the whole role, because a snapshot keeps only the latest row of a series. An acting role or a secondment is a row, and the return from it is another.
  • org.employment.ended closes the period.

The series is org.employment, keyed by org and stint, the way a medicine course is keyed by drug and course. org is one agreed spelling, lower case with words joined by hyphens, the name the person uses for the employer, so the same employer is the same string across every source. stint counts the person's periods with that org from 1. Leaving and rejoining is stint 2, a new series, so the first period stays closed. An employer is not an identity type: the organisation is a string, and the legal entity is a field.

A date is a local date, read in Australia/Sydney wherever the employer is, as the other date types are, with no zone pointer and no source_time, since a date has no clock to read (card 198). date_precision says how much of it the source supports, day, month or year. When it is coarser than a day, date is the first day of that month or year, and the schema refuses any other day, so a month known as "September 2019" is written 2019-09-01. basis says why the date is believed. An end is written as the first day the person was no longer employed, the day after the last day worked, because a date is the start of its day and the stint is closed from then.

No type here has a pay field, and each one's handling says so. The classification is Confidential. The id is declared, <source>:employment/<org>/<stint>/started, the same with ended, and role-changed/<date> for a role, so a start and an end are one each per stint.

Two bundled projections serve them. career is a log over every org.employment. type: each start, change and end at its date. employment is a snapshot of the three types, which answers "who was I working for on that day" when read at a fact-time bound: the latest row of each period still open, with an ended period gone. Concurrent employments each stand. Neither is part of recent-life.

Envelope The one message an importer hands the gate; the only way anything enters the ledger.

term

The Envelope is the message a source hands the admission gate: a type, a definition version, a payload, and optionally a source_ref, an asserted person and a span hint. Over the daemon the source is not a field; the importer's token names it.

It is the only ingress. Importers share nothing with the ledger except through it, which is what lets them be disposable and written in any language. Its wire shape is the batch submission; the Go struct is the envelope.

Where else it is used

  • invariants The Envelope is the only ingress, Admission is three pillars through the one gate
  • mentioned by Anam, a personal longitudinal record, Query
Error kernel The small trusted core that must not corrupt: the append-only ledger plus the gate's admission invariants.

term

Armstrong's term, adopted. Anam's error kernel is the ledger plus the gate's invariants: three pillars, idempotency, immutability. commit writes artifact, anam-event and watermark in one transaction in one file. Everything above it, fold, mint, projections, importers, the CLI, is disposable and rebuildable from source.

Where else it is used

  • invariants commit is idempotent and atomic
  • mentioned by Admission gate, Query
Facet The atomic unit Anam keeps, a source fragment held verbatim plus the metadata that governs it.

term

A Facet is a captured source fragment plus its governing metadata, and deliberately not a normalised domain entity. It has three parts: attributes about itself (id, type, classification, fact-time, series stamp, binding, provenance), a payload descriptor (source schema and version, content hash, payload kind, media type), and the payload, which is either the verbatim structured body or a native reference to a binary in its source.

The payload is opaque to Anam: stored as bytes, never parsed into a canonical schema. Confidence, type and provenance are attributes, never payload fields. A Facet is a value, so its current status is never stored on it; the Fold derives status from anam-events.

A Facet's shape in the ledger is the facet artifact row. Its contract per type is the Facet Definition.

Where else it is used

  • invariants Metadata and payload are separated; the payload is opaque, Anam owns no source fact, Current status is derived by the fold, never stored on the artifact
  • mentioned by Anam, a personal longitudinal record, Fact-time, Ledger, Purchase (the money domain)
Facet Definition The versioned contract for one facet type, from its closed JSON Schema to how identity and fact-time are read from a payload.

term

A Facet Definition is the per-type contract, versioned independently of the facets admitted under it. Its meta names the type, version, identity_key, time_semantics, classification, validation mode and series; its schema is closed JSON Schema draft 2020-12 that declares every field a payload may hold and refuses the rest.

Definitions are governance, not record: they live in the operational store, every version, and never in the ledger. A type is provisioned from one of two places, the JSON bundled in the binary or an importer that registers it, never both. The on-disk format is the Definition manifest.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Definitions live in the operational store, not the ledger
  • mentioned by Attention (the mind domain), Employment (the org domain), Facet, Importer, Intake (caffeine, alcohol and notable meals), Purchase (the money domain), Series
Fact-time When a fact was true in the world; the axis the timeline is ordered by, promoted out of the payload at the gate.

term

Fact-time is when a fact happened in the world: the reading, the night's sleep. It determines where a facet sits on the timeline and which bucket span it belongs to.

It is extracted from the payload at the gate by the Definition's time_semantics.fact_time JSON Pointer, read in the Definition's declared zone, and promoted to a first-class governed field. That promotion is what lets the timeline be ordered and a window filtered without anything opening a payload. Narrowing by fact-time (--from/--to) is a derived span, not an as-of.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Fact-time is promoted out of the payload at the gate, Two clocks, not four
  • mentioned by Anam-time, Bucket span, Query
Fold The computation that derives each facet's current status from its anam-events and keeps the live ones.

term

The fold reads the anam-events up to a watermark and derives each facet's current status (admitted, superseded, tombstoned, endorsed, rejected), keeping the live ones to produce the Canonical. Status is never stored; it is always this derivation.

The serve path is nearest checkpoint plus replay of the events since, so a read is as fresh as ingest. The whole-person view composes bucket checkpoints; it never re-folds the person. The component is fold.

Where else it is used

  • mentioned by Canonical, Facet, Query, Status
Idempotency Re-ingesting the same source row is a no-op; what makes ingest crash-clean and a resend always safe.

term

Same source ref and same content hash is a no-op; the same (facet_id, version) with the same bytes is our own earlier write. Guaranteed by the deterministic facet id and an atomic conditional write, so a worker may crash mid-batch and be re-run to known-good, and an importer unsure whether a batch landed sends it again. See commit is idempotent and atomic.

Where else it is used

  • invariants commit is idempotent and atomic, source_ref is identity; content_hash is content
Importer A ductile plugin outside Anam that reads one source, shapes rows to a Definition, and submits them under its own token.

term

An importer is a ductile plugin in the anam-importers repo that knows one vendor: it reads the native format, shapes rows to the Facet Definition it targets, validates locally, and emits batches that anam_submit posts to the daemon under the importer's token. It composes no identity, joins nothing, summarises nothing, and holds no token itself.

Anam keeps the Definitions, the gate and the record, and knows no vendor. The contract is docs/IMPORTERS.md §8. See the importer base and anam_submit.

Where else it is used

  • decisions A person's words are a note, and a model's reading is a draft, Importers live outside the binary, as ductile plugins
  • mentioned by Anam, a personal longitudinal record, Facet Definition, Snapshot (resend), Source
Intake (caffeine, alcohol and notable meals) What the person says they took in, kept as a fact at its minute so a later question can ask whether it came before a poor sleep or a reading; drinks with a count, meals in the person's words, and no nutrition.

term

health.intake holds an account like "a coffee", "two glasses of wine" or "ate out, rich meal" as a fact at its time. The reason is a question asked later: did it come before a poor sleep, a heart-rate alert or a blood-pressure reading. Before this type such an account was a record.edge note, which no query can count or filter. Nothing here is nutrition. There are no calories, no portion weights and no food database, and Anam does not judge the intake. It is a Definition bundled with the binary, classed Sensitive like the other health types.

kind is caffeine, alcohol or meal. A drink says which one and how many. drink has one agreed spelling, lower case with words joined by hyphens (flat-white, red-wine), the way topic has on attention, because the store never reads inside a payload and a second spelling would be a second drink. count is a whole number from 1. size, volume_ml and, for alcohol, abv are added when the person knows them. Standard drinks or units are worked out on read from those, never stored. A meal says text in the person's words and two yes or no answers, rich and out. The schema is one object with an if and then for each kind: a caffeine or alcohol fact that lacks its drink or count is refused, a meal that lacks any of its three is refused, and a drink carrying a meal's field, or a meal carrying a drink's, is refused too. place is the place's agreed spelling when worth naming; joining an intake to a place fact is an edge's work.

The moment is a wall clock the person said it at, read at the offset source_time names, as for health.medication.dose. When the person says only a part of the day, part_of_day is morning, afternoon, evening or night and at is that part's nominal minute (08:00, 14:00, 19:00, 22:00), so the fact's clock is nominal and not a minute anyone knew. The id is the kind and the minute, then the offset. Two intakes of one kind in one minute are one fact, which is why count carries the number: a coffee tap that sends one coffee sends count 1 at the tap's minute. A different kind in the same minute is its own fact.

The type closes no series. A bundled intake projection serves it as a log, up to Sensitive. To enter it by hand, an operator lists health.intake under the workbench principal's types; an agent saves one through anam-mcp's save_fact.

Ledger Anam's append-only record of everything it admitted, every facet kept and marked at its current status.

term

The ledger is Anam's system of record: an append-only log of every facet ever admitted, with superseded and tombstoned ones retained and marked, plus every anam-event. Facts are added and re-statused, never modified in place, never deleted. It is authoritative about what Anam knew, never about the world; the sources are.

It is one SQLite file of its own, apart from the operational store, with triggers that refuse update and delete for every writer. Forensic reads, as-of reads and rebuild go to it; the Canonical is derived from it. The component is ledger store.

Where else it is used

  • invariants Append-only is enforced by the ledger's triggers, for every writer, Canonical means the live-only value; the ledger means append-everything-marked, The derived tier must stay provably rebuildable from the ledger, The ledger carries no derived indexes
  • mentioned by Canonical, Error kernel, Operational store, Tombstone
Mint Checkpointing per bucket after the importer batches that moved W, rebuilding only the buckets the tail touched.

term

The mint writes checkpoints. It starts from the last recorded run, reads the tail of anam-events since it, rebuilds only the buckets those events touched, splits any over budget, compares each by hash with its head checkpoint, and writes only what changed. A run records its W and seals the log; until a mint seals it again, reads fold in full.

The daemon mints once importer batches that moved W have stopped for 2 s, and at start when the last run is behind the head, each as its own write on the write queue. A full mint (anam checkpoint --full) settles every bucket against the fold and is both the oracle the incremental mint is tested against and the repair. The component is mint.

Where else it is used

  • decisions A mint starts from the last run
  • mentioned by Checkpoint
Operational store The second SQLite file, authoritative for what Anam authors, with each table annotated by where it comes back from.

term

The operational store (tracking.db) holds Anam's own state: Definitions, projection templates, token scopes, the facet and anam-event indexes, the checkpoint log, mint runs, receipts, snapshot parts, and the operator's admission holds. It is a real store, not a cache. Each table has a tier: derived (replay the ledger), provisioned (embedded assets, config, importers registering again), authored (back it up).

Only the derived tier is disposable, and it must stay provably derived: the repair is to delete the file and replay, which cannot reach a fact. It pins the ledger's id and the bucket width it was built at and refuses a mismatch. The component is ops store.

Where else it is used

  • decisions The ledger is its own file, apart from the operational store
  • invariants What Anam authors is authored, never authoritative
  • mentioned by Facet Definition, Ledger
Principal A caller the daemon admits, with a name, a role and the SHA-256 of its token; an importer principal also has the one source it submits as and the types it may submit.

term

Every request that reaches a route carries a principal, found by hashing the presented token and looking the hash up. The operator is the principal whose token is in token_file, named operator; every other principal is in the importers file. The daemon holds hashes, never tokens. A refusal or a log line names the principal, not its token. See principals and roles and role.

Projection A versioned template that prunes the canonical by metadata only; a consumer selects one and never defines one.

term

A projection is a versioned template that prunes the Canonical for a purpose and never reshapes or generates. Its knobs are metadata filters: a facet-type allow-list, a datetime window, an endorsement filter, a maximum classification, and a shape, log or snapshot. It includes or excludes whole facets and never reads a payload to decide. What a reader does with the served facets afterwards, naming fields or running a query, reads the served copy and never widens what the projection admitted.

It is explainable: the verdict that decides whether a facet is served also says which knob excluded it. Served, it is materialised as a content-addressed file with a receipt. Templates are bundled as the projection template.

Where else it is used

  • decisions A read may select by payload; the store never does
  • invariants Projections prune, never reshape or generate; a consumer selects, never defines
  • mentioned by Canonical, Query
Purchase (the money domain) One order or receipt from a merchant, kept as a single fact with its lines and totals as the document states them; the first concept in the money domain.

term

money was one of the four reserved domain heads, and Matt opened it on 2026-10-25 (card 241): "We need a new top level domain, money." That leaves ten heads, seven in use and social, media and work still reserved; see where a distinction is encoded. Purchases are its first concept, and a payment or a transaction would come later as a concept of its own.

money.purchase is a Definition bundled with the binary, written by hand into a records file, entered in the Workbench or saved by an agent. It is an event, Confidential and strict, in the family purchases.

One order or receipt is one facet, never one per line: the lines are items inside it, each with a name and, where the document gives them, a sku, quantity, unit_price and line_total. subtotal, shipping, tax with its tax_label, and total are numbers as the document prints them, in currency, the document's own ISO 4217 code. Nothing is converted: what the bank charged in another currency is a later fact, and so is a refund, each joined to the purchase by an edge.

merchant is one agreed spelling, lower case with words joined by hyphens, the name the person uses for the shop and not its legal entity, which merchant_legal_entity carries. It follows the rule a drug and an organisation follow, so the same merchant is the same string across every source.

The id is declared: <source>:purchase/<merchant>/<order_ref>, so order_ref is required. It is the merchant's reference, verbatim. When the merchant gives none, the writer uses the receipt number, else the date and one word, chosen once, because it is part of the identity. Leave id out and Anam fills it; send it and it must match. The same order written twice is one facet.

date is a local date, read in Australia/Sydney as the other date types are. It carries no source_time, since a date has no clock to read (card 198).

What the purchase was for, whether it is deductible and which financial year it falls in are the person's judgement about it, and they live in the note that joins the purchase, an edge in record.edge, never in this type. The invoice behind it is a record.document facet joined by the same kind of edge, not named in the payload. A schema that refuses fields it does not list keeps deductible out. Card 146 carries the claim as a concept of its own when it is built.

One bundled projection serves the type, purchases, a log up to Confidential. It is not part of recent-life.

Query The third read after the timeline and fields; a Datalog program evaluated over the fold at a watermark, through a projection, answering with a derived value and a receipt. Decided 2026-10-17 and built 2026-10-19 (card 173), reached by anam query, POST /v1/query and GET /v1/query.

term

A query is a Datalog program run over the fold at a watermark, through a projection. The projection decides which whole facets the query may see, as it does for a timeline; the query then relates them: joins across types along fact-time, selection by any payload field the served Definition's schema names, aggregates and windows. Its base relations are the envelope (entry/7, carrying definition_version, since a payload's shape is the shape of the version it was admitted under) and the payload fields read from the served copy (attr/3, for any pointer the schema defines), with closes/1, and month_of/2 and day_of/2 for grouping by UTC month and day. Every evaluation is bounded at 5,000,000 steps. Bundled rules such as current/2, displaced/2 and linked/2 (the edges touching a facet, card 104) ship as a named, versioned rule set, and current/2 is held equal to the snapshot read by a standing test.

The answer is a derived value that hashes, with a receipt naming the watermark and ledger, the projection and its hash, the definition-set version, the rule-set hash and the query text, so receipt verify re-runs it. A query reads payload fields from the served copy and never from the store, moves no watermark and writes nothing; the evaluator is read-side and outside the error kernel. Decided in a read may select by payload; the store never does. Its surfaces are anam query run and describe, POST /v1/query for a program and GET /v1/query for the catalogue a program is written against; docs/QUERY.md is the reference and holds worked questions that a test runs against a real ledger. A query can also carry its projection in the request (card 211): projection takes a template object, with a manifest's knobs and no name or version, in place of a name. The projection loader checks it, a bad one is a 400 that names the field, and it is known by its content hash. The receipt marks it inline and the body is filed under that hash, so verify replays it after a restart. Every role may already read every projection, so it widens nothing. A query builds attr/3 only when the relation it answers reaches it (card 203), so a program over the envelope alone pays nothing for the payloads. The bundled rule set is at version 2 (card 212): it adds in_force/2, and within(A, B, Days) joins it as the first entry in a table of builtins, predicates the evaluator answers in Go because the language has no arithmetic. Every earlier text a receipt may name is kept under rules/history/, and verify chooses the text by the receipt's rule-set hash, never by version, because version 1 was served as three different texts; a receipt whose hash no held text has is a moved rule set and answers 409.

Where else it is used

  • decisions A read may select by payload; the store never does
  • invariants Projections prune, never reshape or generate; a consumer selects, never defines
  • mentioned by A read may select by payload; the store never does, Metadata and payload are separated; the payload is opaque, Projections prune, never reshape or generate; a consumer selects, never defines, Projection
Receipt Proof of exactly what a projection served at a watermark, verifiable from its stored row alone or against the ledger.

term

A receipt pins the served bytes' hash, the exact facet-id set, the watermark and the ledger_id it counts in, the template and definition-set versions, served_at, the span, and since card 87 the query a read narrowed by. receipt verify re-derives the value from the ledger and re-hashes the file.

Publication is recorded here and nowhere else: a facet's published time is the earliest served_at over the receipts naming it. The record is the receipt.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Publication is not an anam-event
  • mentioned by Anam, a personal longitudinal record, Anam-event, Derived span, Projection, Query, Watermark
Role What a principal's token opens, one of reader, importer or operator; each route names the roles it admits and no handler checks who is calling.

term

A reader reads the record and publishes reads. An importer reads as a reader does and alone submits envelopes, documents and Definitions, as its one source and for its types. The operator runs the daemon (minting, backups, projection templates, the inspector) and submits nothing, since it is no source. The three are an invariant of the build, not a setting: see each route names the roles it admits. A reader narrowed to some projections is not built (see the gap). Not to be confused with a source, which is where facts come from.

Where else it is used

  • mentioned by Principal
Series Which facets are repeated statements about one thing, stamped at the gate so a snapshot can keep the latest per thing.

term

A series says which facets are repeated statements about one thing over time. It is neither identity (every change is its own row) nor type (health.medication.started and health.medication.stopped are one regimen). A Definition declares a family, the ordered key pointers that name one series within it, and whether its type closes the series. The gate composes <family>:<values> and stamps it on the facet beside fact-time, so no read opens a payload to group.

Where else it is used

  • mentioned by Fold, Attention (the mind domain), Employment (the org domain), Snapshot (read)
Snapshot (read) The last-known-value read: the latest facet per series at or before a fact-time, closed series dropped.

term

For a fact-time D, the snapshot read keeps the latest facet in each series at or before D, drops a series whose latest is a closing type, and leaves facets without a series alone. It is a pure function of the value it is taken over, so it gives the same answer from the canonical, from an as-of read, or from the composed prefix of hard checkpoints up to D's bucket. A projection with shape snapshot serves it.

The word has a second, unrelated meaning on the importer side: see Snapshot (resend).

Where else it is used

  • mentioned by Query, Snapshot (resend)
Snapshot (resend) An importer's labelled resend-everything run, from which Anam reports what the source no longer holds.

term

An importer never tombstones. A resend-everything run labels each batch as one part of a snapshot (id, part, parts, types); Anam keeps the parts and the facet ids they carried, and once every part has arrived reports what is live from that source, untouched since the run began, and not carried. The operator then retracts with anam retract <source>. A part is bound to its envelopes: resent with others it is a 409.

Unrelated to the snapshot read, which shares the word. The record is the snapshot part.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Retraction by complete resend
  • mentioned by Snapshot (read)
Source A capture origin, named lower-case hyphenated; part of every facet's identity and provenance.

term

A source is where facets come from: bp-csv, withings, garmin, records, documents. It is the identity that appears in provenance, in cursors, and inside the source ref, so an importer that takes over another's facets must use that source's name or it would mint new facets beside the old ones; anam_records and anam_documents kept records and documents when Anam's own workers went (card 119). Over the daemon the source is not an envelope field; the importer's token names it.

Where else it is used

  • mentioned by Role, Source ref
Source ref The deterministic natural key saying which source row a facet is, composed from identity fields and never from values.

term

A source ref identifies which source row a facet is. The gate composes it through one function from the source name and each identity_key value, length-prefixed, so two different tuples can never compose one string. facet_id is uuidv5 of it, so the same row always maps to the same facet.

Identity decides which fact; the content hash decides whether it changed. Braiding values into the ref would give a corrected reading a new facet_id, orphaning the original instead of superseding it.

Where else it is used

  • decisions The gate composes identity from the Definition
  • invariants source_ref is identity; content_hash is content
  • mentioned by Admission gate, Idempotency, Source, Supersession
Status A facet's current state, derived by the fold from anam-events and never stored; distinct from its immutable birth status.

term

Status is one of admitted, superseded, tombstoned, endorsed or rejected, and it is always derived by the fold from anam-events. The artifact stores only its birth status, the state it was born in (admitted or proposed), which never changes. One truth per fact, no place overwritten.

Where else it is used

  • invariants Agent findings are derived facets admitted as proposed; endorsement never writes back, Current status is derived by the fold, never stored on the artifact
Supersession What happens when a source row changes: a new artifact version and a superseded event against the prior, nothing mutated.

term

When a row with a known source ref arrives with a different content hash, the gate writes a new artifact version and appends a superseded anam-event against the prior version. The old artifact stays. Same ref and same hash is a no-op, which is what makes re-sending a batch always safe and a crashed import re-runnable.

Where else it is used

  • invariants Append-only is enforced by the ledger's triggers, for every writer, source_ref is identity; content_hash is content
  • mentioned by Source ref
The whole-person read The person is the substrate and the whole-life read is the default.

term

A life has no edges, and "longitudinal is system-of-record gravity". Anam makes the person the substrate and accepts that gravity at the scale of one life. The whole-person timeline read is the default and normal mode; a span is an optional lens.

What this does not license: the read default is unbounded, the mint unit is not. Minting stays per bucket span.

Where else it is used

  • decisions The person is the substrate
  • invariants The whole-person read is the default; a span is an optional lens
Tombstone A tombstoned anam-event marking a facet retracted without deleting it; never a new artifact.

term

A tombstone is a tombstoned anam-event against a facet's current artifact version. No artifact is written, because an Anam artifact has nowhere to carry a status; the status transition is the whole event. The facet stays in the ledger forever, marked, and is absent from the Canonical. It advances the watermark like any mutation.

Only anam retract writes one; no ingest tombstones, because a row missing from one scan and a source gone look alike from inside an envelope.

Where else it is used

  • decisions A tombstone is an event, not an artifact version
  • invariants An importer never tombstones; the operator retracts, Append-only is enforced by the ledger's triggers, for every writer
  • mentioned by Anam, a personal longitudinal record
Watermark W, the seq of the newest anam-event; the position every checkpoint and receipt is pinned to.

term

The watermark W is the seq of the newest anam-event. It advances with every event, a pure status transition included; if it did not, a checkpoint could reuse a value that still lists a retracted facet. The ledger stores no counter: W is read as the highest seq, and a trigger keeps the sequence gapless.

A watermark means nothing against another ledger, so a receipt carries the ledger_id its watermark counts in. Wᵥ (covers_w) is the watermark a checkpoint actually folded.

Where else it is used

  • invariants W is the anam-event sequence itself: one number, gapless, never stored
  • mentioned by Anam-event, Canonical, Checkpoint, Fold, Query, Receipt, Tombstone

Components (29)

Admission gate The only ingress: resolve binds an envelope to the person, commit validates it against its Definition, composes identity, and appends through the ledger idempotently.

component core basis: code internal/gate/gate.go#New anchor func New(l Ledger, defs Definitions, scopes TokenScopes, clock Clock, personID string, limits Limits) *Gate {

Depends on Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Definitions registry (internal/def), Facet domain values (internal/facet), Fold group: kernel

Two calls, as the doctrine says: Resolve enforces association (binding and its assurance), CommitBatch enforces facet-compliance and token scope and writes.

PillarWhere
Facet-compliantthe Definition's closed JSON Schema, validation_mode, fact-time pointer and zone (or the zone the fact names at its zone_pointer, card 198), series pointers
Authenticatedthe token scopes: a source may inject only the types it is scoped for
Associatedbinding to the one person, with assurance high / medium / review

What commit computes, per envelope: the source ref from the Definition's identity_key (refusing one the envelope carried that disagrees), the facet_id as uuidv5 of it, the fact-time promoted out of the payload, the series stamp, the content hash of the canonical payload. Then the outcome: no prior version is admitted; a prior version with the same hash is no-op; a different hash is superseded with a new artifact version. A batch of up to MaxBatch (49) envelopes is one transaction, so a refused envelope writes nothing for the whole batch. MaxBatch is a constant, not a config key: every importer cuts its batches to it, so a gate set lower would refuse their full batches, and it decides which events share an anam-time (card 151). The payload bound and the retry count are the two gate.Limits the daemon passes in from the config (max_payload_bytes, 300 KiB; commit_retries, 5); see config limits.

The retry on a stale watermark lives here, not in the store, because a worker may already crash mid-batch and be re-run to known-good. Retract writes a tombstone event against the current version and no artifact. Rehearse and RenameBatch (card 140) carry a facet into another type as its next version: the prepared envelope is marked with the type it leaves, nothing on the wire can set the mark, and the disposition supersedes on a type change only under it, with the payload unchanged or not; every other path keeps the refusal of a changed type (card 105). VetCarry and CarryBatch (card 200) admit a facet another ledger holds as itself, held to the Definition version the caller names, which a gate given a Held with Holding resolves; a commit stays held to the version in force, and the API's refusal of an envelope shaped to another version stands.

The gate is half of the error kernel; the other half is the ledger store.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Admit a batch, Hand Anam a document, Migrate to a fresh ledger, Rename a type, Retract from a snapshot
  • decisions A hard-coded value is a default or an invariant, A person's words are a note, and a model's reading is a draft, Fact-time is promoted out of the payload at the gate, Per-source importer tokens are the authenticated pillar, Importers live outside the binary, as ductile plugins, The gate composes identity from the Definition, A tombstone is an event, not an artifact version
  • invariants A batch is at most 49 envelopes, admitted whole or not at all, commit is idempotent and atomic, Agent findings are derived facets admitted as proposed; endorsement never writes back, The Envelope is the only ingress, Metadata and payload are separated; the payload is opaque, Anam owns no source fact, source_ref is identity; content_hash is content, Admission is three pillars through the one gate, W is the anam-event sequence itself: one number, gapless, never stored
  • change paths Change a Definition's shape with a new version
  • depended on by CLI (internal/app), Daemon
  • mentioned by Envelope, Error kernel, Fact-time, Source ref
Fold Derives each facet's current status from its anam-events in seq order and builds the canonical value, sorted and hashed.

component core basis: code internal/fold/fold.go#Fold anchor func Fold(events []facet.AnamEvent) (map[string]State, error) {

Depends on Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Facet domain values (internal/facet) group: derived

Event kindPreconditionState after
admittedunknown facet, version 1v1, admitted
supersededprior version is the current onenext version, admitted
tombstonedconcerns the current versiontombstoned
endorsedconcerns the current versionendorsed
rejectedconcerns the current versionrejected

Live means admitted or endorsed. StatusSuperseded never appears in a state: only an artifact version is superseded, the facet is re-admitted at the new one. A stream that breaks a precondition is ErrCorruptStream.

ReadAt replays the ledger to a watermark, folds, fetches exactly the version the fold derived for each live facet, and builds a Value: fold entries sorted by fact-time then id, serialised once, hashed. The watermark is not in the bytes, so a rebuilt ledger reaches the same hash at a different W.

Snapshot and Fates give the last-known-value read over a series. WatermarkAt translates an anam-time into a watermark for --as-of. Displaced lists every version no longer in force, for --explain.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Migrate to a fresh ledger, Read the timeline
  • invariants Any state is the nearest checkpoint plus replay since its watermark, Canonical means the live-only value; the ledger means append-everything-marked, Current status is derived by the fold, never stored on the artifact
  • depended on by Catch-up (internal/catchup), Admission gate, Inspector (internal/inspect), Mint, Projection, Read (internal/read), Selection (internal/selection), Serve (internal/serve), Operational store (internal/store/ops)
  • mentioned by Fold
Ledger store (internal/store/ledger) The append-only SQLite file: facet artifacts, anam-events and ledger_meta, with triggers that refuse update and delete for every writer.

component core basis: code internal/store/ledger/ledger.go#Open anchor func Open(ctx context.Context, path, personID string) (*Ledger, error) {

Depends on Facet domain values (internal/facet) group: kernel

Three tables, three access paths, no secondary indexes.

TableKeyHolds
facet(facet_id, artifact_version)one facet artifact per version, payload as a BLOB
anam_eventseqone anam-event per change in knowledge
ledger_metakeyperson_id, ledger_id, schema_version and each schema_migration, write-once

The primitive is two calls, not one with an optional artifact: Append writes an artifact with its admitted or superseded event, Mark writes a status event alone. Both run in one transaction that checks, in order, that the head is the watermark the caller read (else ErrStaleWatermark), that this version is not already written (same bytes is ErrAlreadyWritten, different bytes is ErrDivergentArtifact), and that the insert is accepted (else ErrCorrupt). There is no watermark row: W is MAX(seq), and a trigger lets an event take only the next seq.

Create is the one place the schema moves. Open refuses a file pinned to another person or at another schema version. OpenReadOnly opens with mode=ro, so SQLite refuses every write on that handle; every command that only reads uses it. The file runs in WAL mode.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Admit a batch, Migrate to a fresh ledger, Retract from a snapshot
  • decisions Commands that only read open the ledger read-only, The ledger is its own file, apart from the operational store, The substrate is accidental: only internal/store names SQLite, A tombstone is an event, not an artifact version
  • invariants Append-only is enforced by the ledger's triggers, for every writer, What Anam authors is authored, never authoritative, commit is idempotent and atomic, The derived tier must stay provably rebuildable from the ledger, Every external call carries a deadline and fails fast, The ledger carries no derived indexes, Metadata and payload are separated; the payload is opaque, Only internal/store may name SQLite or database/sql, Anam owns no source fact, Sort keys are identity-first, and integers are typed, Current status is derived by the fold, never stored on the artifact, W is the anam-event sequence itself: one number, gapless, never stored
  • depended on by Backup (internal/backup), Catch-up (internal/catchup), CLI (internal/app), Daemon, Fold, Admission gate, Inspector (internal/inspect), Mint, Read (internal/read), Serve (internal/serve)
  • mentioned by Admission gate, Ledger
anam_bp_csv Shapes a blood-pressure app export to health.blood-pressure v8, an Open mHealth body beside the export's own text, one reading sent once from the newest export.

component supporting basis: code ../anam-importers/bp_csv/run.py#handle anchor def handle(request: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:

Depends on Importer base (anam_importer) group: importers

handle takes one export from a folder watch; poll resends every export under export_dir as one snapshot. The CSV reader mirrors Go's encoding/csv, and an export whose record boundaries cannot be read for certain is refused whole. Readings sharing a timestamp are told apart by an ordinal, which with the Excel date is the Definition's identity. Exports order by the date in their name, and a reading two exports hold goes once, from the newer, so a corrected comment stands and a resend is no-op. Traced in admit a batch.

  • invariants An importer shapes payloads and nothing else

anam_submit The one generic plugin that posts batches: makes sure Anam publishes the batch's Definitions, submits under the importer's token, and raises the absent alert on a completed snapshot.

component supporting basis: code ../anam-importers/anam_submit/run.py#handle anchor def handle(request: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:

Depends on Importer base (anam_importer), Daemon group: importers

One instance per importer in the gateway, each with its own token_secret from the vault, so the token names the source and no importer holds it. handle reads a batch event, parses the manifests it carries, calls ensure_published, stamps current versions when the batch says current_versions (the records importer), submits, and emits anam.admitted with the outcome counts. A 422 is a failed run, not retried. When the part completes a snapshot and the report says facets are absent, it emits anam.snapshot_absent keyed on the snapshot id, and retracts nothing. health lists Anam's Definitions. On origin/master (anam card 103) it also posts the bytes of each file a batch's documents names to POST /v1/documents before the envelopes; see hand Anam a document.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Admit a batch, Hand Anam a document, Rebuild the record from its sources, Register a Definition, Resend a source whole
  • decisions Per-source importer tokens are the authenticated pillar, Importers live outside the binary, as ductile plugins, Retraction by complete resend, The importer's client stays on its origin and trusts only a whole receipt, A token names a principal with a role
  • invariants An importer never tombstones; the operator retracts
  • change paths Change a Definition's shape with a new version, Add a source as an importer
  • depended on by anam_documents
  • mentioned by anam_documents, Importer
Buckets (internal/span) Bucket arithmetic over fact-time: the configurable width, self-describing ids, and the layout that subdivides a year into months and days.

component supporting basis: code internal/span/span.go#New anchor func New(w Width) (Buckets, error) {

Depends on nothing in the bundle group: derived

New is the only constructor of Buckets and the only place an unknown width is refused; config calls it at load so a typo dies at startup. Buckets.Of is arithmetic with nothing to fall back to. Ids say their own width: bucket:2026-07, bucket:2026-W28, bucket:2026, bucket:2026-07-03.

Layout is derived from the checkpoint log, never stored: a year is split once a checkpoint of one of its months exists, a month once a day exists. Splits only add. A weekly id does not subdivide a year, so LayoutOf refuses it; see weekly width disables composed reads.

Where else it is used

  • invariants The bucket width is checked once at config load and pinned into the index, Minting is per span, never whole-life
  • change paths Change the bucket width or the checkpoint budget

Catch-up (internal/catchup) Replays anam-events since the index's last seen seq into the operational store, so the derived tier is behind after a crash, never wrong.

component supporting basis: code internal/catchup/catchup.go#Sync anchor func Sync(ctx context.Context, l Ledger, o Ops, buckets span.Buckets) (Result, error) {

Depends on Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Operational store (internal/store/ops), Fold, Buckets (internal/span) group: derived

There is no transaction across the two files, so the index is never written in the same breath as the ledger. Sync reads the index's last_seq, replays the ledger's events after it, folds the state each leaves, stamps each facet's bucket from its fact-time, and writes facet index rows, edge index rows read from each live edge facet's own payload (card 104), and event index rows. An index ahead of the ledger is ErrIndexAhead, which the daemon refuses at start rather than serving from.

The daemon runs it as its first write and after every admission. anam retract does not run it, so after a local retraction the index is behind until the daemon's next start.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Admit a batch
  • decisions One write worker in the daemon, The ledger is its own file, apart from the operational store
  • invariants The derived tier must stay provably rebuildable from the ledger
  • depended on by CLI (internal/app), Daemon, Inspector (internal/inspect)
CLI (internal/app) The anam command tree; every command routes to the daemon when ANAM_SERVER is set, or opens the local files itself.

component supporting basis: code internal/app/app.go#Run anchor func Run(ctx context.Context, info BuildInfo) error {

Depends on Daemon, Client (internal/client), Config (internal/config), Admission gate, Read (internal/read), Mint, Serve (internal/serve), Catch-up (internal/catchup), Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Operational store (internal/store/ops), Inspector (internal/inspect) group: entry

cmd/anam/main.go is thin: it wires build identity and signals and hands off to app.Run. The cobra tree lives here.

CommandOpensRouted to the daemon?
initledger read-write (create)no
daemoneverything, read-writeis the daemon
timeline, changes, coverage, status, receipt, checkpoint, projectionledger read-onlyyes
query run, query describeledger read-onlyyes
query checka real ledger read-only, to hold the bundled current/2 equal to fold.Snapshotno, refuses a configured server unless --ledger names a copy
daemon readynothing: asks /healthz on the configured address and exits by the answer; the container's health checkno, asks the address, not server
move-artifactsthe operational store read-write, to read an old artifact folder in (--yes writes; card 215)no, local only
versionnothingprints the build, and the daemon's when server is set
holdnothing local: the daemon's own admission holdsyes, and it needs the daemon
serveledger read-only, artifactsno, local only
retractledger read-only to plan, read-write with --yesno, refuses to run with a server configured
renameledger read-only to plan, read-write with --yesno, refuses to run with a server configured
migratethe old ledger and store read-only; the new pair created and written with --yesno, takes its files from flags and ignores a configured server
inspectledger read-onlyprints the daemon URL when one is set

Routing is one function, loadRoutedConfig: with server set the command builds a client; without it the command opens the files. Commands that must own the files (daemon, retract, rename, serve, move-artifacts) call loadConfig, which refuses a configured server.

There is no ingest command since card 119. Facts reach the ledger only from importers, through the daemon's POST /v1/envelopes; the CLI writes the ledger only to create it (init), to tombstone (retract --yes), and to carry facets into a renamed type as new versions of themselves (rename --yes, card 140, workflow), and to make a new ledger and carry the live facets of an old one into it (migrate --yes, card 200, workflow). openLedgerForWrite and registryOf live in init.go.

anam hold <source>... puts a source's admission on hold, anam hold --all holds every source, anam hold --release [<source>...] releases them, and anam hold alone lists what is held.

A second binary, cmd/anam-bench, is the read performance bench (internal/bench): gen builds a synthetic record of a named shape through the real gate, run times every read scenario against it, mint mints it as anam checkpoint does, sizes records the facet count of each checkpoint that has none, and compare prints two runs' medians side by side. gen and run load one catalog per invocation from the definitions_dir and projections_dir of the anam config found as anam finds it (--config, card 257), and say which on stderr. It is not part of anam and the container image does not build it.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Migrate to a fresh ledger, Mint checkpoints, Publish a projection, Read the timeline, Rebuild the record from its sources, Rename a type, Retract from a snapshot
  • decisions Commands that only read open the ledger read-only, Every who-may rule is one static policy package
  • invariants The whole-person read is the default; a span is an optional lens
  • change paths Add a read command that works locally and over the daemon
Config (internal/config) Viper over YAML and ANAM_* env vars; validates every path and limit, refuses an unknown key or bucket width at load, and reads the importers file as principals.

component supporting basis: code internal/config/config.go#LoadOverriding anchor func LoadOverriding(path string, set map[string]string) (Config, error) {

Depends on Buckets (internal/span), Facet domain values (internal/facet) group: tools

The keys are the config keys. person_id has no default on purpose: it is pinned into the ledger at init. Validate calls span.New so a misspelt width fails at startup, not on the admission path. LoadPrincipals reads the importers file and refuses a name, a source or a token hash used twice, a role it does not know, and two importers naming a type the binary does not bundle (see principals and roles). Since card 151 Config squashes in [Limits](/data-structures/limits.md), 21 bounds and waits with a Default… constant each, range-checked by Limits.Validate at load; Load and LoadOverriding set them with setLimitDefaults, so any of them can be overridden by a key or an ANAM_ variable (see a hard-coded value is a default or an invariant). The imports file and ResolveImports went with the in-process workers (card 119).

Daemon The one process that owns the ledger on unraid; HTTP routes behind role-checked tokens, a single write queue, catch-up and mint after each write.

component supporting basis: code internal/daemon/daemon.go#Open anchor func Open(ctx context.Context, cfg config.Config, opts Options) (*Daemon, error) {

Depends on Admission gate, Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Operational store (internal/store/ops), Catch-up (internal/catchup), Mint, Read (internal/read), Serve (internal/serve), Definitions registry (internal/def), Config (internal/config), Backup (internal/backup), Inspector (internal/inspect), Library (internal/library), Principals and roles (internal/daemon auth) group: entry

Start

Open runs in this order: check the limits (Limits.Validate), read the operator token (token_file, or refuse a non-loopback listen), build the bucket width, load the importers file as principals (refusing a principal that holds the operator's token, or two importers naming an unbundled type), open the ledger read-write and read-only, open the operational store, check its index is not ahead of the ledger, run guardCatalog (def.Guard over the catalog's Definitions against the ones the store last saw, so a Definition that would re-key the ledger's facets stops the daemon starting), load Definitions (refusing a registered one the catalog contradicts at the same type and version) and projection templates, run persistCatalog to store the catalog's Definitions and their history only once every check has passed, log daemon: catalog admitted, open a read-only ops handle for read routes. The write handle also serves publishing a receipt and saving a selection, two idempotent inserts that take no turn on the write queue. Serve then runs the single write worker, catches up the index as its first write, and mints if behind.

Routes

One table in api.go names each route with the roles whose tokens open it (see each route names the roles it admits). Every role is a reader's for the read routes; the table below says who else.

RouteRolesDoes
POST /v1/envelopesimporteradmit a batch through the gate; see admit a batch
POST /v1/definitionsimporterregister Definitions for its own types; see register a Definition
GET /v1/definitions[/{type}[/{v}]]anypublish Definitions
POST /v1/documents?ext=importer with record.documentstore one document's bytes in the library as <sha256>.<ext> (cards 102, 150): 201 stored, 200 already held, 400 a bad extension, 403 a token without record.document; see hand Anam a document
POST /v1/projectionsoperatorregister a projection template (card 125); a registered version is immutable and every version stays readable for receipts
GET /v1/projections[/{name}[/{v}]]anypublish projection templates
POST /v1/hold, DELETE /v1/holdoperatorhold a source's admission or release it (authored state in the operational store; anam hold): a held source's POST /v1/envelopes and POST /v1/definitions answer 503 with Retry-After, and GET /v1/status lists what is held in held
GET /v1/snapshots/{id}any; an importer sees its own source'sthe resend report: live, absent, carried
GET /v1/timeline, GET /v1/changes, GET /v1/status, GET /v1/checkpointsanyreads, bounded by max_read_bytes
GET /v1/coverage[?w=&as_of=&projection=]anyeach type's live count, earliest and latest fact-time, and each source's share, at the head or a named W, counted from the same value the timeline reads (card 90); says what is recorded, never that a source is complete
POST /v1/receipts, GET /v1/receipts[/{hash}[/verify]], GET /v1/receipts/{hash}/facets/{facet_id}anypublish, verify, and open one served facet beside its links from the edge index (cards 91, 104); a read over max_read_bytes is published once and answered a page at a time, GET /v1/receipts/{hash}?cursor= reading each page from the served value (card 92). The list takes principal, since, until and zone, which is who read what (card 216); a token set to record every read has GET /v1/timeline answered as a publish and opens only the receipts that name it
GET /v1/query, POST /v1/queryanythe query catalogue (what a program may name), and running a program over what a projection serves, with a receipt (card 173); a program is bounded by max_manifest_bytes, and its evaluation by max_read_bytes and 5,000,000 steps (413)
POST /v1/selections, GET /v1/selections/{code}anysave a program and get its short code, and read it back (card 206); a row is inserted whole or not at all, never the ledger, and takes no turn on the write queue
POST /v1/facets/{id}/retractoperatortombstone one facet of the source the body names, on the write queue, with the plan anam retract <source> --facet applies (card 188); 409 for a facet of another source, one not live, or a source an importer declares and has completed a snapshot for
POST /v1/checkpointsoperatormint on the write queue
POST /v1/backupsoperatorVACUUM INTO both stores under backup_dir
POST /v1/entryoperatorhand-enter a fact as the workbench source (never held)
/operatorthe inspector

/healthz and GET /v1/openapi.yaml (the spec of every route above, held to the mux by a test) are outside the table and need no token. A missing or unknown token is 401; a token whose role the route does not admit is 403 naming the principal.

Every unsafe request passes browserGuard first (card 108): a foreign Origin, a Sec-Fetch-Site other than same-origin or none, or on a tokenless daemon a Host that is not a loopback name, is 403 before the token is read and before any work is queued. A cached basic-auth token is exactly the credential a page on another site would spend, so the guard runs ahead of admit.

The daemon reads no source itself (card 119): POST /v1/ingest, GET /v1/operations/{id} and the imports file are gone. GET /v1/status lists each importer's source with last_scan_at, which now means when its last batch arrived, from a sources view in minting.go that is held in memory and forgotten on restart.

The write queue

Every write is one job on one channel, run by one goroutine under the job's deadline. An operator write waits accept_wait (250 ms) to be accepted; an importer's batch waits importer_wait (5 s) and gets a 503 with Retry-After (retry_after, 15 s) after that. The wait spends the request's budget, the shorter of timeout and the caller's Anam-Timeout, so a request that runs out of it before the queue takes it is a 503 and its write never starts (card 110; see queue wait spends the caller's budget). Reads never take a turn; they use the read-only handles. Publishing writes artifacts only and takes no turn either.

The admission hold is read inside the write, not before it is queued: a hold and an admission are then one order, so a batch whose turn comes after a hold is set is refused and a batch whose turn came before it is admitted. A hold the write cannot read refuses the batch too, since an admission that cannot see a hold must not admit.

Mint cadence

An importer batch that changed W requests a mint on a one-slot channel, and mintOnRequest runs mintAtHead as its own write after mint_quiet (2 s) of quiet. At start mintIfBehind requests one when the last run is behind the head. Both live in internal/daemon/minting.go. See mint cadence follows the ledger.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Admit a batch, Hand Anam a document, Mint checkpoints, Read the timeline, Rebuild the record from its sources, Register a Definition, Resend a source whole
  • decisions A hard-coded value is a default or an invariant, Per-source importer tokens are the authenticated pillar, Importers live outside the binary, as ductile plugins, Mint cadence follows the ledger, One write worker in the daemon, Published reads page by a checked cursor, Commands that only read open the ledger read-only, Reads over the daemon are bounded by bytes, Retraction by complete resend, A token names a principal with a role, Every who-may rule is one static policy package

  • depended on by anam_submit, CLI (internal/app)
  • mentioned by Library (internal/library)
Definitions registry (internal/def) Parses Definition manifests, holds the bundled set from embedded JSON, merges registered ones, and guards a new version against re-keying.

component supporting basis: code internal/def/register.go#Register anchor func Register(body []byte, stored, registered []Stored, bundled *Registry) (Registration, error) {

Depends on Facet domain values (internal/facet) group: kernel

FunctionDoes
Bundledthe registry of the 44 manifests under internal/def/manifests, embedded in the binary
Parseone Definition manifest from bytes, refusing an open schema
Guardat daemon start (guardCatalog), refuses a stored version whose identity_key, identity_template or fact_time differs from the bundled one, but for two widenings proved from the stored versions (card 198): a zone_pointer added to the fact time, and optional placeholders appended to the template
Registerdecides an importer's registration: bundled type is ErrBundled unless it is the body the binary carries (unchanged, card 139), same body is adopted, different body at a held version is ErrRegistered, older version is ErrVersionBehind, re-keyed is ErrKeyed

A type lives in one place: while the binary bundles it no importer may register it, and a registered row for a bundled type is shadowed with a log line.

Since 2026-10-13 (card 111) manifests/history/<type>.v<n>.json carries every earlier version a facet may have been admitted under; Manifests() returns them with the current bodies so the start-up upsert provisions the whole history into the operational store, while Bundled() and SetVersion() skip them. Card 157 found that note.annotation v2 had been bumped without copying v1 there (the type's lineage: note.annotation (v1 to v3), then record.note (v1, v2), now record.edge), so a rebuilt operational store would have lost the words the first notes were admitted under; v1 and v2 now both ship in history. A history body is words, not a contract: checked to be JSON naming its type and version, never parsed as one.

Since 2026-10-24 (card 199) a Definition may declare handling.label, a short template of literal text and {/pointer} fields that a page draws a facet of the type as (label.go). It is the one key of handling Anam reads. Parse checks it (a non-empty string, braces closed, at least one field, no {source}, transform or optional mark, every pointer a field the schema declares) and Definition.Label(payload) renders it: a missing or non-scalar field is nothing, a text field is its first line, spaces collapse, and a label whose fields are all empty is "", so the page falls back to the type and the time. A label is part of the version, so health.weight v6, health.medication.dose v3, mind.attention v2, health.intake v2 and record.edge v3 are the versions that added one; a type whose importer shapes envelopes to a fixed version (blood pressure) waits for the importer.

Since card 198 a fact may name its own zone: time_semantics.fact_time.zone_pointer, a field of source_time, is read in place of the Definition's zone when the payload holds it (factzone.go), and the gate refuses an offset the named IANA zone does not keep at that wall clock. source_time has a second shape, sourcetime/v2.json, which adds and requires offset. An identity template placeholder may end in ?, rendering nothing when its pointer is absent. record.edge v2 and health.medication.dose v2 require source_time and read its offset; health.weight v5 declares it, since records sends weight without it.

  • invariants A type lives in one place, A registered Definition version is immutable, source_ref is identity; content_hash is content, Admission is three pillars through the one gate

Facet domain values (internal/facet) The value types everything else speaks in: Facet, AnamEvent, Envelope, the five event kinds, and SourceRef, the one function that composes identity.

component supporting basis: code internal/facet/id.go#SourceRef anchor func SourceRef(source string, values ...string) string {

Depends on nothing in the bundle group: kernel

No dependencies, by design: the domain does not know what it is stored in. SourceRef is the source name followed by each identity value length-prefixed (<bytes>:<value>), so two tuples can never compose one string however many colons a value carries. Canonicalize gives the payload bytes the content hash is taken over. The structs are the facet artifact, the anam-event and the envelope.
