Mint Writes bucket checkpoints after each change, starting from the last run and touching only the buckets the tail dirtied; also the composed reads that start from those checkpoints.

component supporting basis: code internal/mint/mint.go#Run anchor func Run(ctx context.Context, d Deps, dryRun bool) (Result, error) {

Depends on Fold, Buckets (internal/span), Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Operational store (internal/store/ops) group: derived

Two jobs in one package, because they share the layout and the tail logic.

Writing. Run tries fromLastRun: with a budget, a sealed log and a last run, it replays the tail in (R, W], finds the buckets those events dirtied (where each touched facet's version sat at R and sits at W), rebuilds them from their checkpoint plus the tail, splits any over budget along the calendar, and settles each by comparing its hash with the head row. Unchanged buckets stand without being decoded. Then it records the run with a digest of its checkpoints and clears the checkpoints the newest run does not read. RunFull folds the whole ledger and settles every bucket; it is the oracle the incremental mint is tested against and the repair when the log is unsealed, and it writes back any value the log names that the store lost.

Reading. ReadAt loads the leaf checkpoints of the last run at or below W, applies the tail, and gives a composed value with no fold of the whole ledger. ReadWindow decodes only the buckets a fact-time window overlaps and those the tail dirtied, streaming every other bucket's bytes into the whole-value hash. Both return ok=false, and the caller folds in full, when the log is not pinned to this ledger, has no run, holds weekly ids, or can no longer yield the checkpoints the run took (a row or a value is gone). A checkpoint is a cache, and a missing one is a miss.

Traced in mint checkpoints and read the timeline.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Admit a batch, Mint checkpoints, Publish a projection, Read the timeline, Rebuild the record from its sources
  • decisions A mint starts from the last run, Bucket on fact-time, monthly by default, subdivided by a byte budget, Mint cadence follows the ledger, One write worker in the daemon, The person is the substrate
  • invariants Any state is the nearest checkpoint plus replay since its watermark, Minting is per span, never whole-life
  • change paths Change the bucket width or the checkpoint budget
  • depended on by CLI (internal/app), Daemon, Inspector (internal/inspect), Read (internal/read), Serve (internal/serve)
  • mentioned by Mint
Operational store (internal/store/ops) The tracking.db SQLite file: Definitions, the facet and event indexes, the checkpoint log and its values, receipts and saved selections, the operator's admission holds, mint runs, snapshot parts, and the pins that refuse a mismatched ledger or width.

component supporting basis: code internal/store/ops/ops.go#Open anchor func Open(ctx context.Context, path string, buckets span.Buckets) (*DB, error) {

Depends on Fold, Buckets (internal/span), Facet domain values (internal/facet) group: derived

Each table is annotated with its tier in the schema, internal/store/ops/ops.sql, which is embedded.

TierTablesComes back from
derivedfacet_index, facet_edge, anam_event_index, checkpoint (+ span_head view), checkpoint_value, checkpoint_size, mint_run (with its digest of the checkpoints a read takes), syncreplaying the ledger, then minting

Open applies the schema, pins the bucket width and refuses a file built at another, and PinLedger refuses an index derived from another ledger. OpenReadOnly is what the daemon's read routes use. The content-addressed tables are filed and read through Put, Get, Raw, Size and List by kind (see stored content is a row keyed by its hash), RunCheckpoints takes a run's checkpoints and says when the log no longer yields the ones the run saw, and CollectCheckpoints ends a mint by removing the checkpoints the newest run does not read. Checkpoints are a cache. The repair is to delete the file and replay, which cannot reach a fact.

admission_hold holds a source's admission at the daemon's door (POST /v1/hold): a held source's envelopes and Definition registrations are refused, and the workbench is never held. A held row is not rewritten; releasing it deletes it. It is authored state, so anam migrate carries it into the new store like the registered rows.

Data structures here: checkpoint row, facet index row, edge index row, snapshot part.

  • decisions Stored content is a row keyed by the SHA-256 of its bytes, Definitions live in the operational store, not the ledger, Retraction by complete resend, The ledger is its own file, apart from the operational store, The substrate is accidental: only internal/store names SQLite
  • invariants What Anam authors is authored, never authoritative, The bucket width is checked once at config load and pinned into the index, The derived tier must stay provably rebuildable from the ledger, The ledger carries no derived indexes, Only internal/store may name SQLite or database/sql, A registered Definition version is immutable
  • change paths Change the bucket width or the checkpoint budget

Policy (internal/policy) Pure functions over the request and the importers file's principals that answer every who-may question the daemon, the inspector and the CLI ask; it reads no payload.

component supporting basis: code internal/policy/policy.go#Allow anchor func Allow(req Request) Answer {

Depends on Config (internal/config) group: entry

Card 223. Allow takes a route's roles from the daemon's route table and answers allowed, unknown (401) or forbidden (403). ScopesOf is an importer's authenticated pillar, handed to the gate, and MaySubmitType asks the same scopes for a snapshot's declared types and the Workbench's allow-list. MayRegisterType and MayStoreDocument are one test: the principal lists the type. Records, ReaderOf and TimelineRead say how a recording token's reads are filed; MayOpenReceipt gates opening and verifying a receipt; ListScope says which receipts and snapshots a principal's lists show, as rows a per-principal override would join. Declared, MayRetract and MayRetire read the importers file for the sources it declares; whether a declared source has completed a snapshot is the store's part and stays in internal/retract. Held answers the admission hold: the daemon reads the hold rows inside the write and passes them in on the request, and Holdable and MayHold keep the workbench out of every hold. The package imports only config and facet, which a test holds. See every who-may rule is one static policy package.

Where else it is used

  • depended on by Principals and roles (internal/daemon auth)
Principals and roles (internal/daemon auth) Every caller is a principal with a name, a role (reader, importer or operator) and a token hash; admit asks the policy whether the route admits its role, with 401 for an unknown token and 403 for a role the route does not admit.

component supporting basis: code internal/daemon/auth.go#admit anchor switch policy.Allow(policy.Request{Caller: who, Known: ok, Roles: roles}) {

Depends on Config (internal/config), Definitions registry (internal/def), Policy (internal/policy) group: entry

Card 89. The daemon holds a map from the hex SHA-256 of a token to a config.Principal, built once by loadPrincipals at Open. The operator comes from token_file and is named operator, a name no other principal may take; everyone else comes from the importers file. The daemon never holds a client's token, only its hash, and a principal whose hash equals the operator's is refused at start.

RoleOpens
readerevery read, and publishing a read, which writes artifacts and never the ledger
importera reader's routes, and submitting envelopes, documents and Definitions as its one source and for its types only
operatora reader's routes, and minting, backups, projection templates and the inspector; it submits nothing, since it is no source

who finds the principal for a request (a bearer token, or a basic-auth password so a browser can open the inspector). On a daemon with no token_file, which listens on loopback only, a request that presents no known token is the operator's. admit wraps each route with the roles its entry names in the route table (see each route names the roles it admits), asks policy.Allow, and puts the principal in the request context; handlers read it with principalOf and ask internal/policy every question about it, branching on nothing else (see every who-may rule is one static policy package). Besides the role, the policy reads the principal's receipts setting, which is data in the importers file as the role is: a token set to always has its timeline reads and queries filed as receipts that name it (card 216). The 403 names the principal, its role, the route and the roles the route admits.

A token set to record every read must name a projection to read the timeline, since a receipt is what a projection served. It opens, verifies and lists only the receipts that name it, so no read of facets goes unrecorded; asking GET /v1/receipts for another reader's is 403 (card 223). A dry_run query files nothing for any token: the receipt log covers real reads, and there is no rule against a dry run. If the receipt cannot be filed the read fails and returns nothing. The operator and every other token list, open and verify any receipt.

An importer sees only its own source's snapshots; a reader or the operator sees any. Both list rules are the policy's ListScope. /healthz is outside the table and answers without a token.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Per-source importer tokens are the authenticated pillar, A token names a principal with a role, Every who-may rule is one static policy package
  • invariants Each route names the roles it admits, The Envelope is the only ingress
  • depended on by Daemon
  • mentioned by Config (internal/config), Daemon, Importers file, A reader is not narrowed to projections, Principal
Projection Applies a template, bundled or registered, to a canonical value, metadata filters only, and explains each facet's verdict by the knob that decided it.

component supporting basis: code internal/projection/projection.go#Explain anchor func Explain(v fold.Value, t Template, g Gate, s Series) ([]Verdict, error) {

Depends on Fold, Facet domain values (internal/facet) group: published

Knobs, applied in this order so the first failing one names the reason: type allow-list (an exact type, or a prefix ending in a dot that admits every type under it at a segment boundary, matched by facet.Matches; card 193), status filter, classification ceiling, fact-time window, then the consent gate (consent.Open returns allow today; a nil gate is refused), then the shape. Shape snapshot runs over what the other knobs admitted and demotes entries to displaced-by or closed-by using fold.Fates. The payload is never read. Templates are the three embedded projection templates, bp, sleep and meds, the floor, plus those the operator registers over POST /v1/projections (card 125, 2026-10-13): the Registry holds every version of each by name, Lookup answers the newest and Version the one a receipt names, and Register refuses a changed body at a held version, a version behind, or a type with no Definition.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Publish a projection
  • invariants Canonical means the live-only value; the ledger means append-everything-marked, Metadata and payload are separated; the payload is opaque, Projections prune, never reshape or generate; a consumer selects, never defines
  • change paths Add or change a projection template
  • depended on by Inspector (internal/inspect), Read (internal/read), Serve (internal/serve)
Read (internal/read) The read API behind timeline, changes, publish and the snapshot report: parses the query, picks composed or full fold, narrows, and bounds the answer.

component supporting basis: code internal/read/timeline.go#Timeline anchor func Timeline(ctx context.Context, src Source, q api.TimelineQuery) (api.Timeline, error) {

Depends on Mint, Fold, Projection, Serve (internal/serve), Definitions registry (internal/def), Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Operational store (internal/store/ops) group: derived

Source is the four handles a read needs: ledger, checkpoints, checkpoint values, Definitions. Timeline chooses: a bare window with checkpoints to hand takes mint.ReadWindow; a projection or --at takes the whole value; --explain drops the checkpoints and folds in full so it can list displaced versions. Absent computes what a complete resend did not carry. FitTimeline measures the wire encoding and refuses a read over max_read_bytes with the parameters that would narrow it. Publish is the daemon's receipted read; it files the whole value once and answers its first page, and ReceiptPage reads every page from the served value by a cursor it checks against the receipt (card 92). Every read narrows through one predicate, receipt.Query.Admits, the one a verify replays. Query runs a Datalog program through a projection with a receipt; its evaluation, Evaluate, is exported for the inspector's selection page (card 199), so the page and POST /v1/query answer one program alike within max_read_bytes.

Traced in read the timeline.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Migrate to a fresh ledger, Publish a projection, Read the timeline, Rename a type, Retract from a snapshot
  • decisions Published reads page by a checked cursor, Reads over the daemon are bounded by bytes, The person is the substrate
  • invariants Any state is the nearest checkpoint plus replay since its watermark, The whole-person read is the default; a span is an optional lens
  • change paths Add a read command that works locally and over the daemon
  • depended on by CLI (internal/app), Daemon, Inspector (internal/inspect), Selection (internal/selection)
Receipt (internal/receipt) The receipt record and its proofs: validate, deterministic bytes, and compare a re-derived value with what the file holds.

component supporting basis: code internal/receipt/receipt.go#Receipt anchor type Receipt struct {

Depends on nothing in the bundle group: published

No dependencies: a receipt must be readable from its stored row alone. Compare reports two independent proofs, reproduced (the re-derived hash and id set match) and intact (the stored bytes still hash to served_hash), with missing and unexpected ids. The record is the receipt.

Where else it is used

  • decisions Publication is not an anam-event
  • depended on by Serve (internal/serve)
Serve (internal/serve) Publishes a projection: canonical at head, prune, write the served value then its receipt, and verifies a receipt by re-deriving.

component supporting basis: code internal/serve/serve.go#Publish anchor func Publish(ctx context.Context, d Deps, t projection.Template, w ledger.Watermark, q *receipt.Query,

Depends on Mint, Fold, Projection, Receipt (internal/receipt), Operational store (internal/store/ops), Ledger store (internal/store/ledger) group: published

Run serves a whole template; Publish files value then receipt, so a crash between them leaves a value no receipt names and never a receipt without its value; Narrow applies a read's query after the template; Verify re-derives at the receipt's watermark and refuses a moved template version or another ledger; List and Show read the receipt and served_value tables, which are the only receipt index; List takes a ListFilter of principal and served-time window, applied to each parsed receipt. Deps.Reader is the principal a receipt filed through the Deps names, empty for every ordinary publish. Traced in publish a projection.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Publish a projection
  • decisions A read may select by payload; the store never does, Publication is not an anam-event
  • invariants Projections prune, never reshape or generate; a consumer selects, never defines
  • change paths Add or change a projection template
  • depended on by CLI (internal/app), Daemon, Read (internal/read)
anam_documents Sends a folder of documents with .meta.yaml sidecars as one snapshot of record.document envelopes, each batch naming the files whose bytes anam_submit hands Anam's library before the envelopes go.

component detail basis: docs docs/INBOX.md#Documents as facts of their own anchor reads the share's `documents/` folder, and `anam_submit` posts each file's bytes to

Depends on Importer base (anam_importer), anam_submit group: importers

Anam's documents worker moved out (anam card 103), kept under the source name documents so a resend of the live library is all no-op (card 119). Every regular file under documents_dir is one document; dot-files, dot-directories and sidecars are skipped. Its fact-time comes from the sidecar <file>.meta.yaml, which must say date: YYYY-MM-DD and may say more (title, note); a file without one stops the run and names the sidecar to write. The sidecar is read with the records importer's YAML reader, so both resolve a scalar as Go's yaml.v3 did.

Identity is the folder-relative path: a record.document's path is the document's name, and its sha256 is how Anam finds the bytes, so a rescan under the same path supersedes the facet and the first bytes stay in the library. The importer measures sha256, bytes and media_type from the file, and a sidecar that declares any of them is refused. Each anam_documents.batch event carries at most 49 record.document envelopes, current_versions, and a documents: [{path, file}] list. anam_submit reads each file and posts its bytes to POST /v1/documents?ext= before it posts the envelopes, so only Anam writes the library and no bytes ride in an event. Anam names the stored file <sha256>.<ext>; on anam-importers branch card-150-sha256-library, Client.store_document(name, data) takes the extension from the name's suffix (card 150). See hand Anam a document.

The plugin is documents/ in anam-importers, which has its own explainer; this page cites Anam's own account of the worker it replaced.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Hand Anam a document
  • invariants The Envelope is the only ingress
  • mentioned by Importer base (anam_importer), Batch event
anam_dreamstation Decodes a CPAP SD card copy with the dreamstation package and sends every session as health.cpap-session.dreamstation, a type the binary does not bundle, so it registers its own Definition.

component detail basis: code ../anam-importers/dreamstation_card/run.py#batch_events anchor def batch_events(request: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:

Depends on Importer base (anam_importer) group: importers

Every run sends the whole card, old sessions come back no-op. The run is labelled a snapshot only when the decoder skipped no file; an events file whose summary is not on the card belongs to a session the run cannot send, so that run carries no label.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Register a Definition
  • invariants An importer shapes payloads and nothing else
  • mentioned by Importer base (anam_importer)
anam_records Sends a records folder as one snapshot of the bundled types it carries, reading YAML with Go's yaml.v3 rules so every record Anam's own records worker admitted before card 119 resends as no-op.

component detail basis: code ../anam-importers/records/run.py#poll anchor def poll(request: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:

Depends on Importer base (anam_importer) group: importers

It carries no Definitions and does not validate locally: every type it sends is bundled in Anam, and each batch says current_versions so anam_submit stamps the version Anam publishes. Plain scalars resolve as Go resolves them (yes stays text, 0777 is 511, 1_000 is 1000), and a number Python would write differently from Go stops the run, because the gate keeps a number's text and a different one would supersede. The one-off extractors under records/extract write records files from exports that will never be sent again.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Resend a source whole
  • invariants An importer shapes payloads and nothing else
  • mentioned by Importer base (anam_importer), anam_documents
Backup (internal/backup) POST /v1/backups copies both SQLite files with VACUUM INTO and the artifact and library directories into a dated folder, keeping backup_keep of them.

component detail basis: code internal/backup/backup.go#Take anchor func Take(ctx context.Context, d Deps) (api.Backup, error) {

Depends on Ledger store (internal/store/ledger) group: tools

Runs as a write on the daemon's queue, so no admission is mid-flight. Each store copies itself in one read transaction; a .partial directory is renamed on completion; older sets are pruned to backup_keep. There is no anam backup CLI command, only the route; and the ledger's .backup advice in config.example.yaml names a different method (see config example is stale).

Where else it is used

  • workflows Rebuild the record from its sources
  • depended on by Daemon
  • mentioned by Library (internal/library), config.example.yaml still names the old backup method and a stale store list
Client (internal/client) The Go HTTP client the CLI uses against the daemon: bearer token, a timeout header, a network margin, and a bound on the answer it reads.

component detail basis: code internal/client/client.go#New anchor func New(server, tokenFile string, timeout, margin time.Duration, maxBody int) (*Client, error) {

Depends on nothing in the bundle group: entry

One method per route the CLI uses: Timeline, Changes, Status, Mint, Checkpoints, Publish, receipts and projections. Receipt reads every page of a receipt and joins them, so it answers the whole value as it always has; ReceiptPage reads one page by its cursor (card 92). Every request carries the token_file token as a bearer (the operator's, unless the file holds another role's) and an Anam-Timeout header; the daemon takes the shorter of that and its own timeout, and the client itself waits that plus network_margin (10 s by default). An answer over max_response_bytes (64 MiB by default) is refused with "narrow the read". Both numbers reach New from the loaded config; the Anam-Timeout header name is a constant because both ends of the wire must spell it alike (card 151). The wire types are internal/api, shared with the daemon, so the CLI and the daemon cannot disagree about a field name.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Mint checkpoints, Read the timeline
  • decisions A hard-coded value is a default or an invariant, Published reads page by a checked cursor, Reads over the daemon are bounded by bytes
  • invariants Every external call carries a deadline and fails fast
  • change paths Add a read command that works locally and over the daemon
  • depended on by CLI (internal/app)
  • mentioned by CLI (internal/app), Config limits
Inspector (internal/inspect) The web harness over the full fold, the Anam Workbench: timeline, facets with the edges touching them, events, projections, the selection builder with its saved codes and definitions, the default library's bytes confined to its root, forms that enter, correct and retract a fact by hand, and the link form that joins facets with words.

component detail basis: code internal/inspect/inspect.go#Handler anchor func Handler(d Deps) http.Handler {

Depends on Fold, Projection, Definitions registry (internal/def), Buckets (internal/span), Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Mint, Operational store (internal/store/ops), Library (internal/library), Selection (internal/selection), Read (internal/read), Catch-up (internal/catchup) group: tools

A diagnostic tool that also writes (see "Entering a fact by hand" below), and it keeps the full fold rather than reading from checkpoints. Routes: /, POST /view (the query / opens on by default, or none), /facets, /facet, /location (the live place.track hours in a window), /events, /projection, /select, /select/save (card 206), /document?sha256=<sum>&name=<file name>, /definitions, /definition, and /importers (what the importers file declares beside what each source last sent, and the route table). With a Workbench: /entry, /retract and /link, below. /document finds the file by its sha256 with library.Find and opens it under an os.Root on the library, so a symlink that points out gets the same 404 as a sum that is not a sha256 or a file that is not there. The name, the base of the reference's path, only names the download in Content-Disposition (card 150). Mounted inside the daemon as the operator role's route (the inspector shows the whole record unprojected, so no reader or importer token opens it), or standalone with anam inspect. The number of historical as-of folds it keeps is inspect_cached_reads (4 by default, 0 keeps none), a bound on its memory however many instants a person clicks through (card 151).

Since 2026-10-13 (card 21) the timeline carries a checkpoint strip under the band: one segment per leaf bucket of the mint's layout, sealed, behind or never minted by the one hash comparison of the bucket's value at W against the log's head, computed for the page and dropped. The daemon hands the inspector its read-only ops handle for that; anam inspect over a bare ledger draws no strip.

Since 2026-10-13 (card 106) every library document is served in a CSP sandbox with neither scripts nor the origin, and HTML, XHTML, SVG and XML files are downloads rather than inline previews: a document is untrusted material, and the inspector shares its origin with the API, so an active file shown inline could have read the record as the operator.

Links on the facet page

Since 2026-10-21 (card 104, slice 3) the facet page carries a links section: the live edges touching the facet, each with its kind, which side the facet is on (from, to or both), the moment it was asserted, every facet id on each side as a link, the edge itself as a link, and for a note (kind about) its text. The page reads them through the same code the API's GET /v1/receipts/{hash}/facets/{facet_id} answers from, read.LinksOf over the edge index (facet_edge in the operational store), so the page and the API cannot drift. The daemon hands the inspector its read-only ops handle as Edges, the way it hands Checkpoints, wrapped so that an index a catch-up has found wrong refuses with inspect.ErrIndexWrong, the one word of "wrong" the inspector knows: the facet page then refuses as the API's facet route does, and the timeline, which is the ledger's and stays right, keeps the page and draws the checkpoint strip as one red band carrying the refusal, since none of the log can be trusted until the operator rebuilds the store; anam inspect over a bare ledger is given none and the page says there is no index to read, which is not the same as no edges. The edges carry their own watermark, the index's cursor when they were read, shown beside the heading, as in the API's answer. The text is the edge facet's own, read from the ledger by the edge's id, since the index holds ends and not words. Links are never written into a facet: the payload stays the entirety of the facet, and the page draws the derived part beside it.

The projection page

Since 2026-10-21 (card 201) GET /projection?p=<name> opens on a summary, not on every row. It draws the header (watermark, the canonical and served hashes, the projection and as-of controls) and one line per facet type with three counts, served, excluded and superseded. Served and excluded come from projection.Explain over the live value the page already holds, so the verdict words are the ones anam timeline --explain gives. Superseded comes from the fold's per-facet state alone: a facet at version n has n-1 earlier versions, and a facet that is no longer live has its current version out of force as well, which is the count fold.Displaced lists, reached without reading a version from the ledger. A renamed facet's earlier versions count under its current type, because the fold does not hold what type an earlier version had. A facet that is no longer live has no type in the fold, so those are counted together on a line named (not live). Each count is a link that opens its rows.

The rows view is ?p=<name>&type=<type>&show=served|excluded|superseded, with from and to as dates on fact time (both ends included, days in the reader's zone) and asof as before. It draws that type's rows of that standing, earliest first, at most 500, and when more match it says "Showing the earliest 500 of N" and asks for a narrower window. Only the superseded standing reads versions from the ledger, through fold.DisplacedWhere limited to that type's facets; the page before card 201 read all of them for every projection and drew about 45,000 rows. The as-of control works in both views and the links keep it. An unknown show or a date that is not YYYY-MM-DD is a 400.

The selection page

Since 2026-10-21 (card 199) GET /select builds a Datalog selection with the shared filter (the selection component). The program is the value; the criteria and the boxes are a view of it. Every state is an address, so back and reload keep it:

  • p, the projection the selection reads through, canonical by default, chosen as on the projection page; the value is the one serve.Derive serves, the query read's own.
  • zone, an IANA name: a bare date is a day in it (card 236, below).
  • from and to, each a date in that zone (a to date is the whole day) or a whole fact-time, which is how a hand-edited bound off midnight stays a criterion; type and source, each repeated; standing, live unless superseded.
  • tick, the basket, repeated; fromside, the ticks on the from side (to is the default); untick, the exclusions.
  • program, a hand-edited program, in place of all of the criteria and the basket.

The criteria form adds any criterion in any order, each criterion is a chip with the count the criteria up to it match and a link that removes it, and the matches are listed by type, the readings of one type, source and fact time folded into one row with a box per facet. A box is ticked while the program picks the facet. The boxes post back to the page, which answers with a redirect to the address of the new state: a match left unticked becomes an exclusion, and "keep the ticked in the basket" makes each ticked match a tick, so it stays when the criteria change. With more than 500 rows the page lists none, states the count and asks for another criterion; a derivation over max_read_bytes is refused the same way. The program shows below in a textarea. A submitted program that selection.Parse reads redirects to the address of its criteria; any other runs as text through read.Evaluate, the same evaluation POST /v1/query makes without its receipt, within the same max_read_bytes, and the page turns the criteria and boxes off and says why. Besides the save below, the page only reads. "Use this selection" links to /link with the program, the projection, the basket with its sides, and edit, the link being edited when the selection was opened from one, which every address of the page carries.

Since 2026-10-24 (card 199) each member of a row reads as its label when the type's Definition declares handling.label (the definition component), with the short id kept beside it, and the basket and the unticked list show the label before the type and time. A type without a label keeps the short id in the row and the type and time in the basket. labels.go renders them from the served value's payloads under the Definitions in force, clipped to 80 characters.

The projection page's rows view selects its type, window and standing through the same selection.Filter, so the two pages admit by one predicate.

Dates in the reader's zone

Since 2026-10-24 (card 236) a bare date is a day where the reader stands on every Workbench page that reads one, the rule the API, the CLI and anam-mcp already kept (clock.ParseBound, which read's own parseInstant now calls). The select page, the projection page's rows view, the timeline's from/to and the location page take a zone parameter, an IANA name. Before, the select and projection pages turned a date into UTC midnight (selection.Days) and the timeline read a bare date as UTC (parseInstant), so "from 12 Oct" in Sydney started at 11:00 on the 12th and missed the 07:30 blood draw that morning. A request without a zone reads in the daemon's configured zone (Deps.Zone, from zone or ANAM_ZONE, UTC when that is Local with no name to send); an unknown zone, and Local, are a 400 that names the zone.

The zone shows in an editable field beside the dates. While nothing on the page has been read in a zone (no from, to, sel or program) and the request named none, the field carries data-fill and the page's script (readzone.html) replaces its value with the browser's zone, as the entry form fills a fact's zone; the form then sends it. Without script the field keeps the daemon's zone. A page that already read a date keeps the zone it read it in. The zone parameter is part of a stored default view.

A bound is written back as a date only when its instant is midnight in the zone (dayIn), a to as the day before the exclusive midnight, and otherwise as the instant, so from=2026-10-11T20:30:00.000000000Z stays whole. The selection page's program carries the zone as a comment, and a saved selection keeps the instants it was saved with and says the zone its dates were read in, from that comment, or UTC for a selection saved before it. The projection page's as-of stays UTC: it is an anam-time, not a day.

Handing a range between the timeline and the select page

Since 2026-10-24 (card 237) a zoomed timeline offers "select in this range" and the select page offers "show on timeline", each a link to an existing route (rangehandoff.go). The first carries the window as from/to, the type filter and the zone to /select; an unzoomed timeline sends no range. The second opens / at the filter's from/to, its types and the zone when a date is written, and sel when the page was opened at a saved selection, which the timeline then draws as it does today; a filter with nothing to say opens /?view=whole. A bound is a date when it is midnight in the zone and an instant otherwise, so a Sydney day stays a Sydney day both ways. The source filter does not travel, since the timeline has none, and a type prefix is expanded to the types the record holds because the timeline filters by exact type. No new route and no state.

Saved selections

Since 2026-10-22 (card 206) a selection has a short code (and since card 236 its page names the zone its dates were read in). "Save this selection" posts the program to POST /select/save, which files it through the daemon's selection.Store (the selection component) and lands on /select?sel=<code>: the code shown as sel:<code> in a read-only box to copy, with links that open it on the timeline, highlighted on the timeline, and in the link form. Saving writes an artifact and never the ledger. The route exists only when the inspector is handed Selections, as the daemon does; a standalone anam inspect keeps none, and answers a code with a 404 page that says so.

?sel=<code> opens a saved selection on three pages. The selection page draws the chips from it when it is within the grammar, or runs it as text. The link form fills its basket from it as from program. The timeline (/?sel=<code>) runs it through read.Evaluate over the canonical's facets whose fact time lies in the window, within max_read_bytes, and draws only the facets it picks, the window fitted to them when none was chosen; with selmode=highlight it draws every facet and fades all but the picked, which are ringed, each in a lane of its own so a run never hides one. The header reads "selection sel:<code>, N facets", with a link to the other mode, the select page and the timeline without it, and every timeline link, the legend form and the default view carry sel and selmode. Over the bound the header says so and asks for a narrower window, which runs the program over less. A code nothing was saved under is a 404 page, never an empty timeline; a string that cannot be a code is 400.

Entering a fact by hand

Since 2026-10-20 (card 188, slice 4) the inspector is also the Anam Workbench: it can enter a fact, correct one and retract one, through the gate. The daemon hands it a Workbench (declared in internal/inspect/workbench.go) only when the importers file declares a workbench principal; a standalone anam inspect is given none and has no entry routes at all. The interface answers the source the daemon enters as, the types it may enter (the principal's types, so the pages ask the daemon and never read the file), and the two writes: Enter, which ends in the same function POST /v1/entry calls, and Retract, the same as POST /v1/facets/{id}/retract. The read pages gain no write branch; the three routes below are their own handlers.

  • GET /entry?type=<type> draws the form from the type's Definition in force, at request time. A type off the list is 403. Each schema property becomes one control: a string a text box, a number or integer a number box, a boolean or an enum a select, the fact-time field a date or date-and-time box that the server reads in the Definition's zone and writes in its layout. description and examples are the hint and the placeholder, minLength and exclusiveMinimum and the like are hints only (the gate checks again), and handling.guidance is shown word for word. The field the identity_template composes is left out: the gate fills it. A schema with a nested object or a list, such as blood pressure's, gets a plain message and no form.
  • POST /entry reads the posted fields back through the same schema walk that drew them (entryform.go), so a field drawn is a field read, and sends the typed payload with the Definition version the form was drawn from. The answer is the gate's: admitted, superseded, no-op (each linked to the facet), or its refusal words beside the form with everything typed kept. A form drawn from an old version is refused by the gate and drawn again from the version in force.
  • GET /entry?type=<type>&edit=<facet id> opens the same form filled from a live facet the Workbench entered, with the identity fields (identity_key and what the identity_template reads) read-only: changing one would name a different fact, so a wrong time is fixed by retracting the entry and entering a new one. Saving writes a new version of the same facet.
  • POST /retract tombstones one facet of the workbench source after the page's confirm. The facet page shows Edit and Retract on the newest version of a live facet of that source and on no other.

The allow-list is the principal's types; the pages draw no form and offer no link from the type's kind, family or classification. The browser guard wraps every one of these routes, since a form post is same-origin and a foreign page's is not.

The link form

Since 2026-10-22 (card 202) the Workbench writes a link (record.edge) by hand. The entry form cannot draw one, since its sides are lists, so the link form is its own pair of routes, mounted with the entry routes and like them only when the daemon hands over a Workbench, and refused (403) unless the workbench principal's types list record.edge.

  • GET /link draws the form. Its query string carries the basket: tick, the facet ids, repeated; fromside, the ones on the from side; and optionally program with p, the selection page's program and projection, which the form resolves to facet ids once, when it opens, through read.Evaluate within max_read_bytes. kind chooses the kind of a new link. edit=<facet id> opens a live link the Workbench entered, its sides, kind, words, pins and moment filled in; with a basket as well (the selection page handing an edit back) the basket replaces its sides. Each facet shows its label, type, fact time and a link to its page, a box that keeps it in the link, its side, and "link to this version", which pins the content hash of its current version, read through the ledger's CurrentVersion. Pins start off for kind about and on for any other. The kind is a select of the Definition's enum. The moment, at, starts at the browser's now, and the zone and offset are filled from the browser by the same script the dose form uses. A basket that puts a facet on the from side of kind about gets a plain message; more than 20 facets draw a confirmation box.
  • POST /link saves one record.edge through Workbench.Enter, the dose form's write, so it lands as source workbench with the Definition version the form was drawn from. Before the gate, the form refuses (422, nothing written, everything posted kept) a kind off the list, a from side on kind about, more than 20 facets unconfirmed, no moment or offset, and a pin whose version is no longer current (it moves the pin to the current version for the person to check). A new link's id is workbench:edge: and the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of kind=<kind>\nfrom=<sorted from ids, comma-joined>\nto=<sorted to ids>\nat=<at><offset>, computed once at the first save (edgeID in linkform.go); the words are not in it. Each side is written sorted, so the same link saved twice is the same content and the gate's no-op. An edited link keeps the id it was first saved with, so saving it is a new version of the same facet even when its sides change, and a facet removed from it no longer lists it. record.edge declares no identity_template, which would compose the id from the edited sides again and make an edit a second link.

The facet page offers "link this…", which opens the selection page with that facet ticked, whenever the Workbench may enter record.edge; the timeline's own selection (ctrl-click and shift-drag marks, kept in the page) gains a "link these…" anchor under the same condition, which the page's script points at /select?tick=… with one tick per mark, and neither entry point is a new route; on a live link the Workbench entered, Edit reads "edit this link" and opens /link?edit=; Retract is the same as for any Workbench facet. The Definitions pages' Enter for record.edge opens /link.

Where else it is used

  • decisions A hard-coded value is a default or an invariant, Every who-may rule is one static policy package
  • depended on by CLI (internal/app), Daemon
  • mentioned by Daemon, Library (internal/library), Config limits, Hand Anam a document
Library (internal/library) Anam's store of document bytes: files named <sha256>.<ext>, written only by Anam, placed by a folder limit and found again by a walk for the name.

component detail basis: code internal/library/library.go#Store anchor func Store(library string, limit int, sum, ext string, body []byte) (bool, error) {

Depends on nothing in the bundle group: published

A document's bytes, as POST /v1/documents hands them in (cards 102, 150). Store names the file <sha256><ext>, writes it to a nonce-named temporary file with O_EXCL under an os.Root on library_dir, syncs, and renames it into place. Bytes the library already holds, under any extension, are not written again, so the answer is only whether they were new. Other bytes are another name, so a stored file is never overwritten or renamed.

Placement: a new file goes in the library root until the root holds library_folder_limit files (default 1000, ANAM_LIBRARY_FOLDER_LIMIT), then into the subfolder sha[:1]/, then sha[:1]/sha[:2]/, and so on. Only regular files that are not dot-files count; subfolders do not. A stored file never moves, so where it sits depends on when it came, and nothing records it.

Find and Index look a file up by its name: one walk of the library, skipping dot-files and dot-directories (temporaries on their way in) and any file not named by a sha256. IsSHA256 is the lower-case hex test both callers use. The daemon calls Store from postDocument on the write queue and Index from checkReferences; the inspector calls Find to serve /document?sha256=. Backup copies the directory whole. See hand Anam a document.

Where else it is used

  • depended on by Daemon, Inspector (internal/inspect)
  • mentioned by Daemon, Hand Anam a document
Selection (internal/selection) The shared filter: fact-time from and to, type or prefix, source, standing, and facets ticked or unticked by hand, as one Go value that compiles to a Datalog pick/1 program and parses back only when the program is one it writes; and the store that saves a program under a short code.

component detail basis: code internal/selection/program.go#Program anchor func (f Filter) Program() (string, error) {

Depends on Facet domain values (internal/facet), Fold, Read (internal/read) group: derived

Card 199, 2026-10-21. selection.Filter holds the criteria a person adds in any order and the picks they make by hand: From and To as fact-time lexemes (From included, To not, Days(from, to, loc) turns two dates into the bounds of whole days in the reader's zone loc, UTC when it is nil, by clock.Day, the rule every bare date follows, card 236), Types as type patterns (an exact type or a prefix ending in ., card 193), Sources, Standing (live by default, or superseded), Ticks and Exclusions by facet id. It is a set: Canonical sorts each list, drops repeats and drops an exclusion that is also a tick.

The filter has two readings, and TestEquality_ProgramIsThePredicate holds them equal over a real ledger, through the canonical and through the bp projection:

  • The direct predicate. Matches(Row) asks the criteria alone; Picks(rows) is the selection, the rows the criteria match minus the exclusions, plus every tick. A filter with no criteria picks its ticks and nothing else, not the whole record.
  • The program. Program writes Datalog over the query surface's entry/7 that defines pick/1. Each pair of a type and a source the filter names is one rule, a conjunction of the criteria, so the rules' union is "any of these types, any of these sources". A prefix is a pair of lexical bounds, T > "activity.exercise-session." and T < "activity.exercise-session/", which is facet.Matches's own rule, as the bundled edge rules bound a pointer. The standing superseded is St != "admitted", St != "endorsed"; live writes nothing, since a served value holds only versions in force. A tick is the fact pick("<id>"); an exclusion is the fact unpick("<id>"), which each rule reads as not unpick(E). The program reads no attr, so it never names a pointer.

ProgramIn(zone) is Program with one comment line under the header, % dates read in Australia/Sydney, when the filter has a bound and the zone is an IANA name (card 236). The bounds are instants and mean the same in any zone; the line records what the dates meant to the person who chose them, and ZoneOf reads it back (empty for a program that names none, or a zone this machine cannot load, or Local). Program writes none.

Parse reads a program back to the filter only when it is one Program writes, rule for rule: layout, comments and the order of the rules do not matter, anything else does. It extracts a candidate filter, writes that filter's program, and compares the two sets of rules; a join, a recursion, an attr, a constant in an entry slot or a bare date is ErrNotChips, and the error names the line. Parse(f.Program()) is f.Canonical() for every filter Validate accepts (TestRoundTrip).

The consumers resolve the program differently. The inspector's selection page draws the criteria from it and runs it through read.Evaluate, the evaluation POST /v1/query makes, without a receipt. The projection page's rows view (card 201) selects its type, window and standing by Matches. The link form (card 202) resolves it to ids once, when it opens, and migrate (card 200) will keep a query receipt beside its run.

Saved selections and their codes

Since 2026-10-22 (card 206) selection.Store (saved.go) saves a program under a short code, over the operational store's Put, Get and List. CanonicalText is what a program is saved and hashed as: ProgramIn of what Parse reads off it and the zone ZoneOf reads off it, when it is within the grammar (so the zone line survives a save), so another layout or rule order is the same selection, and otherwise the text trimmed. Save refuses text that is empty or does not parse (ErrNotProgram) and files the canonical text as an artifact of kind selection, addressed by its SHA-256. The code is the first CodeLen (8) hex digits of that hash, written sel:<code>.

When another saved selection already shares those 8 digits, the new code lengthens to the shortest prefix of its hash no other saved selection shares, and the lengthened code is filed first, as a small JSON row of kind selection-code ({"code", "hash"}): which of two colliding selections came first is a fact the hashes cannot say, so it is written down once. The first keeps its 8 digits. Saving again answers the code already given and writes nothing new. Open resolves a code: a filed code names its selection; 8 digits name the one saved selection starting with them that did not lengthen; a longer code that was never filed is read as a prefix of a hash. No match is ErrNoCode, two is ErrAmbiguous, and a string that cannot be a code is ErrBadCode. The test finds two tick programs whose hashes share f7c90470 and holds that the later one's code is f7c90470d and both still open.

A code names the program, not its results: as the record grows the same code may select more. The rows are authored, not derived, so a backup carries them with the receipts; a selection's bytes are its Datalog text.

Where else it is used

  • depended on by Inspector (internal/inspect)
  • mentioned by Inspector (internal/inspect)

This is the path the whole design serves: a vendor-shaped row leaves the importer as a payload only, and everything that makes it a fact (identity, fact-time, series, hash, status) is decided at the gate from the Definition. Re-running any step is safe: the same export resends as no-op, a crashed batch is re-sent whole, and a crashed mint is caught up by the next one.

What the trace shows that the docs state in the abstract:

  • The source is never a field. The importer's token names it, so an importer cannot write another source's facets.
  • Two policies for definition_version: the daemon requires it, the gate would still accept 0 as unstated, a policy left from the in-process workers that no caller uses since card 119.
  • The index and the snapshot part are written after the ledger commit, in the other file. A crash between them leaves them behind, and a resend repairs both.
  • The importer trusts nothing short of a whole receipt, because a 200 with a truncated body once looked like success.

Related: the gate composes identity, commit is one transaction, importers live outside the binary.

  • change paths Add a source as an importer

Before card 102 the library was a folder Anam's documents worker read in place, and something outside Anam put the files there. Now the bytes come in over the importer's token like the envelopes do, so the library has one writer and a reference can be checked against the bytes it names. Card 150 named each stored file by its sha256, so a changed document no longer needs a new path: its new bytes are a new file and its facet supersedes. Where a file sits is the library's own business and never enters a fact. The inspector serves a reference's bytes by sha256, confined to the library (see inspector).

Related: the Envelope is the only ingress, importers live outside the binary.

Migrate to a fresh ledger anam migrate --from old --to new --yes carries the live facets of one ledger into a new ledger through the gate, each under the type's current Definition version when it comes out the same, otherwise under the one it was admitted under, so ids, hashes and fact times are the old ones and anam-time restarts at the cut.

anchor is selected now and was not

Card 200. The alternative, re-importing every source into a fresh ledger (the rebuild), needs each importer's source and resends what the ledger already holds; this reads the ledger. A facet's id is its source and identity values and its content hash is over its payload alone, so carrying it unchanged through the gate gives the id and hash it had. The fact time is read from the payload by a Definition version, so the carry holds a facet to the current version only when the facet built comes out identical, and otherwise to the version it was admitted under.

Anam-time restarts: every facet is admitted at the cut, one event each, so the new ledger's W is the number of facets carried and no as-of read before the cut can be answered from it. The old pair, kept read-only, answers those, and every old receipt. The operational store catches up by replay when a daemon starts on the new ledger.

The gate's concession is scoped by the call, as the rename's is. CarryBatch and VetCarry resolve the Definition from a Held the gate was given with Holding; a gate built without one refuses a carry, and nothing on the wire sets a version, so the API's refusal of an envelope shaped to another version stands.

A facet admitted under a version that does not parse as a contract today cannot be held to it, so the plan tries the current version, and refuses the facet only if that fails too. The 1,600 live health.weight facets from records are admitted under v1, whose words use the retired lenient mode (card 52). Their payloads pass the current version with the same id, hash and fact time, so they move to it, flagged "moved from v1" (Matt, 2026-10-22). The proof after --yes holds identity to id, content hash, fact time and status, and reports the moves apart.

The mint's only job is to bound replay; freshness comes from the ledger. The incremental path costs its tail, the buckets the tail touched and a size per checkpoint value. The full mint remains as the oracle it is tested against and the repair for an unsealed log.

The read is where the mint pays for itself: a window read decodes only the buckets it overlaps and the ones a late correction dirtied, and still reports the whole-person hash. The fallback to a full fold is not an error path but the oracle: every composed read is tested equal to it at every watermark.

Without a window (anam timeline bare) the read takes mint.ReadAt, which loads every leaf checkpoint and applies the tail. With --projection or --at the whole value is read and then pruned. With --explain the checkpoints are dropped on purpose so displaced versions can be listed.

Related: any state is nearest checkpoint plus replay, reads over the daemon are bounded, weekly width disables composed reads.

7outsideMintPOST /v1/checkpoints mints the new ledger's checkpoints, and the operator compares counts by type with the drill's report. A fact an operator had retracted while its source still holds it comes back live, so it is fixed at the source first. ends——internal/mint/mint.go#Report

Anam owns no facts, so a ledger can start again from nothing (card 141). The drill (scripts/rebuild-drill.py in anam-importers) proves it without touching the record: it runs each importer's poll and anam_submit into a scratch daemon on a new ledger and compares the result with the live one, type by type. The first run, on 2026-10-14, matched 2,939 facets in 18 types by facet id, content hash and status.

What a rebuild cannot bring back. What only the ledger held. Every fact is admitted at the time of the rebuild, so an as-of read before it, and every receipt naming the old ledger's W, has nothing to read in the new file; the old file keeps them. A source resends what it holds now, so superseded versions are gone. A fact retracted because its source dropped it stays gone; a source retired with --retired sends nothing, so its facets do not return. Whether a rebuild should keep the old anam-times and superseded versions (card 140's migration importer would) is open in IMPORTERS §6. The live rebuild itself, as opposed to the drill, has not been run (see the drill is the only rebuild so far).

Related: resend a source whole, any state is a checkpoint plus replay, the ledger is its own file.

This is the importer contract's "a type lives in one place" made mechanical. While the binary bundles a type, no importer may register it and any number may submit it; when a release drops the type, the one importer naming it takes it over by registering the same body, and two importers naming an unbundled type refuse the daemon's start.

Related: a type lives in one place, Definitions live in the operational store, Definitions are registered on a 404, not at start.

7CLI (internal/app)Admission gateRehearse runs every pillar a commit would, against the new type's Definition, with the facet's source, binding and payload as stored, then decides the disposition against the ledger as it stands, and writes nothing. The prepared envelope carries the type the facet is leaving, which nothing on the wire can set. ——internal/gate/rename.go#Rehearse
anchor p.renamedFrom = r.From
8—Admission gatedisposition refuses a commit whose facet is held under another type (card 105). A prepared envelope marked with the type it leaves, when the facet's current type is that one, supersedes instead, whether or not the payload changed: the hash is over the payload alone, so an unchanged payload would otherwise read as a no-op. The API path carries no mark. ——internal/gate/commit.go#disposition
anchor if p.renamedFrom != "" && current.Type == p.renamedFrom {
9—CLI (internal/app)Per pair, how many facets the gate would carry and how many it would refuse, and its selector; a line for each pair whose type arrived at reads fact time by another rule, with how many of its facets move; then a line per refusal and per unmatched or doubly matched facet, naming the facet and the reason. An unplaced facet or a refusal anywhere ends the run with nothing written, even with --yes. ——internal/app/renamerun.go#writeRenameReport
anchor FROM\tTO\tCARRY\tREFUSED\tSELECT

11CLI (internal/app)Admission gateRenameBatch prepares a block of at most MaxBatch renames, marks each with the type it leaves, and writes them as CommitBatch does: the head read once, one transaction, retried on a stale watermark. Each facet gets its next version under the new type, the new Definition's classification and series, its own source, and the run as injector; the old version keeps its seq. branch: with --yes——internal/gate/rename.go#RenameBatch
anchor ps[i].renamedFrom = renames[i].From
12—CLI (internal/app)The error says how many were carried of how many, and to re-run. A facet already carried sits under its new type, so the fold does not list it again, and the run again plans only what is left. ——internal/app/renamerun.go#renameAll
anchor re-run to finish
13—Read (internal/read)A read as-of a W before the run is unchanged, old types and old projections; a read at the newest W shows the new types. Receipts cut before the run still verify. The operational store catches up by replay. ends——internal/gate/rename.go#Rename
anchor Anam-time is kept

Card 194 lets a pair carry a select, so one table splits a type into leaves, as card 195's renames/vendor-leaves.json splits activity.exercise-session by its payload id's prefix, garmin:session:, concept2:session:, technogym: and strong:session:. The source alone would not do, since Strong and Technogym both arrive through the records importer. A leaf spells its shared core out in full, because a manifest cannot reference another file; internal/def/shared-core.json names the pointers a split's leaves share, and a def test holds their schemas identical, description and examples aside.

Card 195 also lets the type arrived at read fact time by another rule. The keying check had compared the fact-time rule as well as the identity key, as Guard does across the versions of one type. The rule places a facet and does not name it, and the gate reads each carried facet's fact time afresh from the type it arrives at, while the facet's earlier versions keep theirs, so a read as-of before the run is unchanged. activity.day.garmin uses it: it reads the start of the day Garmin counted, where activity.day read the date at Sydney midnight, so the run moves the days the watch spent at another offset, 54 of 717 when it was planned (Matt, card 197).

Card 140. The alternative, a new ledger built facet by facet, flattened anam-time to the migration moment and split receipts from their ledger; this keeps one ledger and adds one version per facet. The type is not in a facet's id, so the new version lands on the same facet; type is a column on every version row, so nothing is overwritten and the append-only triggers permit the insert. The one concession the gate makes is scoped by the call, and ErrTypeChanged guards every other path as before.

Retraction is a command, never a side effect of ingest: a row missing from one scan and a source gone look alike from inside an envelope, so no ingest tombstones. The guards exist because a tombstone cannot be undone by a resend; a resend of the same row is a no-op.

The retired variant (card 120) is the cut-over tool: once the new importer's complete snapshot reports 0 absent and the old source's declaration is removed, anam retract health-db --retired --yes --max-fraction 1 tombstones every live facet of the old source, and a read as of before still shows them.

Related: retraction by complete resend, retract runs locally, not over the daemon.

  • mentioned by Retract runs against the local files, never the daemon, and leaves the index behind

Data structures (19)

Admission response What POST /v1/envelopes answers: w, changed, one positional result per envelope, and snapshot progress; or a 422 naming every refused envelope.

data-structure supporting basis: code internal/api/importers.go#Admission anchor type Admission struct {

relationtocardinality
answersBatch submission1:1
StatusFieldMeaning
200wthe watermark after the batch, or the head read when every envelope was a no-op
200changedadmitted plus superseded
200results[]{index, outcome, facet_id, artifact_version}, outcome admitted, superseded or no-op
200snapshot{id, parts, parts_admitted, complete} when the batch was a part
422error, refused[]{index, type, reason} for every failure; nothing written
400errornot one submission
409errorsnapshot part mismatch or changed
413errorbody over the bound
503error, Retry-After: 15another write held the queue past importer_wait (5 s), or the request's own budget ran out first; the header is retry_after

The importer's client accepts a 200 only when w is a positive integer, changed equals the count it can see, and there is exactly one result per envelope with a known outcome; anything less is retried as a transport fault.

  • decisions The importer's client stays on its origin and trusts only a whole receipt

Anam-event row One row per change in Anam's knowledge: a gapless seq, one of five kinds, the facet and artifact version it concerns, and anam-time.

data-structure supporting basis: code internal/store/ledger/ledger.sql#anam_event anchor seq INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK (seq >= 1),

relationtocardinality
concernsFacet artifact rown:1
ColumnMeaning
seqINTEGER primary key; a trigger allows only MAX(seq)+1, so it is gapless and W = MAX(seq)
kindadmitted, superseded, tombstoned, endorsed, rejected; nothing else
facet_idthe facet it concerns

The gate writes admitted and superseded through AdmitBatch and tombstoned through Mark; endorsed and rejected have no writer yet. The index of these in the operational store, anam_event_index, is what anam changes reads, ordered (anam_time, seq).

Batch submission The JSON body of POST /v1/envelopes: 1 to 49 envelopes and an optional snapshot label; any unknown field is a 400.

A submitted envelope:

FieldRequired

There is no source field. The body is bounded at 49 × max_payload_bytes plus 1 MiB (about 16 MB by default) and must be exactly one JSON value. Checkpoint log The operational store's checkpoint, mint_run and checkpoint_size tables: one row per mint of a bucket, the runs that checked every bucket with a digest of the checkpoints they took, and the sizes.

data-structure supporting basis: code internal/store/ops/checkpoint.go#CheckpointRow anchor type CheckpointRow struct {

relationtocardinality
names by hashCheckpoint value row1:1
TableColumnsMeaning
checkpointspan_id, v, covers_w, value_hash, minted_at; PK (span_id, v)one row per mint of a bucket; span_head is the view of the latest v per span
mint_runw, ran_at, picksa run that checked every bucket at W; a read starts from the last one at or below its W. picks is a digest of the checkpoints the run took (span, version, value hash): a read that cannot take the same ones declines and folds in full
checkpoint_sizespan_id, v, facetsthe count, so an undecoded bucket can still be counted

All derived, and a cache that may go missing: a mint removes the rows the newest run does not read, and a read that finds one gone folds past it. Replay the ledger and mint again. The layout (which years are split into months, which months into days) is read from the ids, never stored. meta beside them pins schema_version, bucket_width and ledger_id.

Definition manifest The JSON contract for one facet type: meta with identity, time, classification, validation mode and series, and a closed JSON Schema 2020-12.

data-structure supporting basis: code internal/def/def.go#manifest anchor type manifest struct {

meta keyMeaningDefault
facet_typethe type; a bundled file must be named <type>.jsonrequired
definition_versionat least 1; envelopes must name the current onerequired
classificationNormal, Confidential, Sensitiverequired
validation_modestrict (schema as written) or verbatim (no schema)required
source_schema, schema_version, media_type, payload_kindthe descriptor copied onto each facet1, application/json, verbatim
time_semantics.fact_time{pointer, layout, zone}: a JSON Pointer, a Go time layout, an IANA zonerequired
identity_keyJSON Pointers, in order, composed into the source refrequired, at least one
min_binding_assurancethe floor a binding must meetmedium
series{name, key[], closes}none
kindwhat sort of fact the type records, for readers: measure, reading or event (card 176); the gate ignores itnone
familythe lower-case hyphenated word a reader groups the type under (meds, sleep); the inspector draws the type on that row (card 176); the gate ignores itnone

Schema rules at load: top-level object with properties; every required property refuses null and the empty string; the top-level field behind every keying pointer is required; every object sets additionalProperties, except a branch of if, then, else, allOf, anyOf, oneOf or not, which narrows the object it sits in and closes nothing (card 233); no $ref leaves the manifest; nothing is fetched.

A version that only loosens a rule leaves older values valid: note.annotation v3 (card 157) made about optional, so v1 and v2 notes stay valid and a note may name no facet yet, because an account can come before the observation it describes. Identity, mechanism and fact time did not change (v2 timed a note by when it was written, not by the moment it is about). A renamed type starts again at v1 under its new name, so the type's lineage is note.annotation (v1 to v3), then record.note (v1, v2), now record.edge (cards 147 and 191), and that body is record.edge v1; v2 (card 198) requires source_time and is read at its offset.

Type names follow one rule (card 147): domain.concept[.specialisation…], with the head one of a closed set of ten, health, activity, place, record, mind, org and money in use and social, media and work reserved. A new fact joins one of the ten, and a head is added only by a logged decision, as mind was (card 222), org (card 239) and money (card 241); growth is in the concept, in a specialisation after it, and in family; see a distinction goes in the cheapest place that lets its reader ask cleanly. A Definition declares kind and family for readers (card 176).

Where they live: 44 bundled under internal/def/manifests, embedded (three of them the retired health-db types, kept so their facets still read), with the 90 earlier versions a facet may have been admitted under in manifests/history. A bundled type may also be carried by an importer, which re-sends the same body at the same version and is answered 200 unchanged (card 139 piece 1). Importers carry the rest under <importer>/definitions/ in the same format and register them: place.stay, place.track, activity.day.garmin, health.cpap-session.dreamstation, health.measurement.withings (vendor leaves since card 195) and record.link, a file Anam remembers but does not hold (card 165). Stored in the operational store's definition (bundled, every version) and registered_definition (immutable) tables.

Where else it is used

  • workflows Admit a batch, Rebuild the record from its sources, Register a Definition, Rename a type
  • decisions A person's words are a note, and a model's reading is a draft, Health measures use Open mHealth bodies where one exists, A distinction goes in the cheapest place that lets its reader ask cleanly
  • invariants A registered Definition version is immutable
  • change paths Change a Definition's shape with a new version, Add a source as an importer
  • referenced by Batch event, Envelope, Facet artifact row, Importers file
  • mentioned by Definitions registry (internal/def), Facet Definition

Facet artifact row One row per artifact version in the ledger's facet table: the governing metadata as columns, the payload as an opaque BLOB, and no status column.

data-structure supporting basis: code internal/store/ledger/ledger.sql#facet anchor PRIMARY KEY (facet_id, artifact_version)

relationtocardinality
admitted underDefinition manifestn:1
ColumnMeaning
facet_iduuidv5 of the source ref; identity, never fact-time
artifact_versionINTEGER, orders numerically; version n needs n-1 beneath it (trigger)
type, confidence, classification, definition_versionattributes about itself
fact_timefixed-width UTC text, promoted out of the payload at the gate
seriesthe Definition's series stamp, empty when the type has none
binding_person_id, binding_assurance, binding_span_hintthe association pillar's result

Triggers refuse update, delete and an insert over an existing key. The Go value is facet.Facet, built only by facet.New, which re-derives the id and hash from the bytes so a facet cannot disagree with itself. There is no status column; a test pins that the struct has no status field. ledger_meta beside it pins person_id, ledger_id, schema_version and each schema_migration:<n>, write-once.

Fold entry One live facet in a canonical value: its governing metadata, derived status and payload, with no artifact version and no watermark so the hash is stable across rebuilds.

data-structure supporting basis: code internal/fold/value.go#Entry anchor type Entry struct {

relationtocardinality
is the live version ofFacet artifact row1:1
JSONFrom
facet_id, type, fact_timethe artifact
seriesthe artifact; omitted when empty so old hashes do not move
source, source_refprovenance
statusthe folded state: admitted or endorsed in a value

payloadthe raw canonical bytes

Deliberately absent: the artifact version (a superseded chain collapses) and seq or W. A Value is entries sorted by fact-time then facet id, serialised once, hashed once; checkpoint values, served values and the timeline all carry these.

Where else it is used

  • referenced by Checkpoint value row, Receipt, Timeline response
  • mentioned by Fold

Snapshot tables The operational store's record of an importer's resend-everything run: the run with its started and completed watermarks, each part's digest, and the facet ids the parts carried.

data-structure supporting basis: code internal/store/ops/ops.sql#snapshot anchor CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS snapshot (

TableColumnsMeaning

Tier disposable (a fourth word beside derived, provisioned and authored): incomplete runs older than snapshot_ttl (a day) are dropped on the next part, a source keeps its snapshot_keep (two) newest complete runs, and a lost table comes back with the next resend. Edge index row The operational store's derived index of each live edge facet's ends, one row per facet id on each side, written by catch-up from the edge's own payload and rebuilt by replaying the ledger.

data-structure detail basis: code internal/store/ops/ops.sql#facet_edge anchor CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS facet_edge (

relationtocardinality
indexes the ends ofFacet artifact rown:1
joins for the edge's fact-timeFacet index rown:1
ColumnMeaning
edge_idthe edge facet's id, a record.edge whose payload joins facets (cards 104, 191)

sidefrom or to: which list of the payload the facet id came from; about, the v1 name, is the to side
facet_idone facet id the edge names on that side

The primary key is (edge_id, side, facet_id), and an index by facet_id answers "the live edges touching X" in one lookup. Catch-up writes the rows in the same transaction as the facet index row and the cursor: an edge's rows are replaced whole at each of its versions and gone when it is tombstoned, so a crash leaves the index behind, never wrong, and a store replayed from genesis holds the same rows as one caught up batch by batch. The query surface needs no table: the bundled linked/2 derives the same relation from the served payloads.

Read by GET /v1/receipts/{hash}/facets/{facet_id}, which answers the entry as the receipt served it beside the edges touching it at the index's own watermark.
