Queue wait spends the caller's budget up to acceptance A request's whole time is the shorter of the daemon's timeout and the caller's Anam-Timeout; waiting for the write queue spends it, a write whose caller has gone or whose budget is spent never starts, and an accepted write is reported as it happened.

invariant supporting basis: code internal/daemon/queue.go#tryWriteWithin anchor busy := time.NewTimer(min(wait, budget))

Applies to Daemon, One write worker in the daemon, Admit a batch

Enforced at internal/daemon/queue.go#run (anchor if !started.Before(until) {)

Tested by internal/daemon/queue_test.go#TestQueueWaitSpendsTheCallersBudget, internal/daemon/queue_test.go#TestQueueRunRefusesASpentBudget, internal/daemon/queue_test.go#TestQueueDisconnectAfterAcceptance

Card 110. Before it, a write waited up to the queue's wait and then ran under a deadline counted from when it began, so the time in the queue was not part of the request's budget. Now tryWriteWithin takes the request's budget as an end time counted from arrival: the wait to be accepted is min(wait, budget), so a 10 ms batch facing an occupied queue is a 503 in 10 ms, not after importer_wait; accept_wait for an operator's write and importer_wait for an importer's, and the write gets what is left. A request that runs out of budget before the queue takes it is ErrBusy, a 503, and its write never started. A caller that goes away first gets its context's error, and its write never starts either.

run checks both again at the moment the worker takes a job, because a select that finds the queue free and the caller's deadline expired at once may pick either. After acceptance the write runs on the daemon's context to its own deadline and its outcome is waited for whatever the caller does, since an answer given before it ended would misreport a write that went on to happen. Write, for the daemon's own long writes (a backup, a replay), starts its deadline when the worker takes the job, so time in the queue does not spend a long write's time. See one write worker in the daemon and every external call carries a deadline.

Where else it is used

  • decisions One write worker in the daemon
  • mentioned by Daemon, One write worker in the daemon, Every external call carries a deadline and fails fast
Sort keys are identity-first, and integers are typed An artifact's key carries facet_id then artifact_version, never fact_time; version and seq are INTEGER columns so they order numerically; times are fixed-width UTC text.

invariant supporting basis: code internal/store/ledger/ledger.sql#facet anchor PRIMARY KEY (facet_id, artifact_version)

Applies to Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Facet artifact row, Anam-event row

Enforced at internal/store/ledger/ledger.sql#facet (anchor PRIMARY KEY (facet_id, artifact_version))

ARCHITECTURE §12, eighth bullet, and §8. Key an artifact on its fact-time and a corrected row whose fact-time moved lands at a fresh key: the prior-version lookup misses it, commit concludes "never seen", and writes v1 again, two live artifacts for one id. Identity-first makes it unrepresentable. A version key built as text orders v10 < v9; typed columns remove that.

§12 still says "every integer in a sort key is zero-padded", a holdover from the DynamoDB substrate; the schema comment says typed columns need no padding (see architecture doc has stale sentences).

source_ref is identity; content_hash is content The natural key never contains a row's values, or supersession silently becomes duplication; the gate composes identity from identity_key and refuses an envelope that disagrees.

invariant supporting basis: code internal/gate/commit.go#prepare anchor ErrIdentityMismatch, env.SourceRef, ref)

Applies to Admission gate, Definitions registry (internal/def), Source ref, Supersession, Idempotency

Enforced at internal/gate/commit.go#prepare (anchor ErrIdentityMismatch, env.SourceRef, ref))

Tested by internal/gate/gate_test.go#TestCommit_TypedRejections, internal/gate/batch_test.go#TestCommitBatch_SupersedesAlongsideNoOps

ARCHITECTURE §12, seventh bullet, and §6a. facet_id = uuidv5(source_ref), and source_ref is the source name plus each identity value length-prefixed. The same source row always maps to the same facet; a changed row hashes differently and supersedes; an unchanged row is a no-op. Braiding a value into the key would give a corrected reading a different id, so it would enter as a new facet and orphan the original.

Enforced at the gate: an envelope may leave the ref out, and one that carries a ref other than the composed one is refused. Keyed by the Definition: a new version may not change identity_key.

The derived tier must stay provably rebuildable from the ledger The ledger is the system of record; the library's files hold documents; the operational store's derived tables come back by replay and must never acquire a second home for a payload, an event or W.

invariant supporting basis: docs internal/store/ops/derived.go#ApplyReplay anchor return fmt.Errorf("ops: replay cursor moved since it was read at %d", fromSeq)

Applies to Operational store (internal/store/ops), Catch-up (internal/catchup), Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Ledger

Enforced at internal/catchup/catchup.go#Sync (anchor case int64(head) < last:)

Tested by internal/fold/rebuild_test.go#TestRebuildFromSource_IdenticalCanonical, internal/catchup/catchup_test.go#TestSync_IncrementalAndIdempotent

ARCHITECTURE §12, fourth bullet, and §8. The rebuild test drops the derived tier and re-derives it from the ledger; if a facet payload, an anam-event or W ever acquired a second home there, the test would pass on data it never rebuilt and "Anam owns no source fact" would stop being falsifiable.

What enforces it in code: the replay cursor is a position, not a watermark, and moves only in the same transaction as the rows it accounts for; an index ahead of the ledger is refused at daemon start; the index holds a facet's folded state and hash, never its payload.

The Envelope is the only ingress Importers share nothing with the ledger except through an envelope posted to the daemon; only an importer's token opens the submit routes, and since card 119 no code in the binary reads a source.

invariant supporting basis: code internal/daemon/daemon.go#Handler anchor mux.Handle(rt.pattern, d.admit(rt.roles, rt.handler))

Applies to Admission gate, Daemon, Importer base (anam_importer), anam_documents, Principals and roles (internal/daemon auth), Envelope

Enforced at internal/daemon/api.go#routes (anchor f("POST /v1/envelopes", importerOnly, d.postEnvelopes),)

ARCHITECTURE §12, last bullet, first half, and AGENTS.md (share-nothing). It is the HTTP contract: facts reach the record only through POST /v1/envelopes, and only an importer's token opens it. The route table gives the importer role POST /v1/envelopes, POST /v1/definitions and POST /v1/documents; the operator submits nothing, since it is no source, and a reader reads and publishes receipts. Since card 89 an importer's token also reads, as a reader's does, and sees only its own source's snapshots, so the old wording that an importer "cannot read the record" no longer holds; share-nothing is about writes (see each route names the roles it admits).

Until card 119 the binary also held internal/capture, two in-process workers for records and documents, and check.sh failed the build if one imported internal/store. Both moved to importers and the package is gone, so the gate has one admitting caller, the daemon's batch route (anam retract uses the gate only to tombstone). A document's bytes come in beside the envelopes, through POST /v1/documents, but they are not a fact: the record.document envelope that names them is, and it is refused unless the library holds those bytes (see hand Anam a document).

The ledger carries no derived indexes Anything derived lives in the operational store; the ledger serves three access paths, two on a primary key and one on its own lookup index, anam_event_by_facet, for the gate's fold of one facet.

invariant supporting basis: docs docs/ARCHITECTURE.md#Invariants anchor The ledger carries **no derived indexes**

Applies to Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Operational store (internal/store/ops), Ledger

Enforced at not enforced

ARCHITECTURE §12, eighth bullet, last sentences, and §8. The three paths: one facet's versions highest first (the current version commit compares against), the anam-events in a seq range (replay since a watermark), and one facet's events by anam_event_by_facet on (facet_id, seq), the lookup the gate's retraction check folds from (card 65). That index is on the ledger's own rows and serves the kernel's write path, so it is a lookup, not a derivation. The timeline in fact-time order, a type filter and changes_since are derived, and live in tracking.db. The index is installed as its own step from internal/store/ledger/indexes.sql (card 221) and a read-only open does not create it.

Until 2026-10-22 the doc said "no secondary indexes" while the code held this one; see the ledger has one secondary index, now closed.

The whole-person read is the default; a span is an optional lens Storage is partitioned by the person onto one timeline; no span is required to read, and a derived span only narrows a read.

invariant supporting basis: code internal/read/timeline.go#Timeline anchor case narrow(src, q, b):

Applies to Read (internal/read), CLI (internal/app), Derived span, The whole-person read

ARCHITECTURE §12, third bullet, first half. anam timeline with no flags reads the whole canonical; --from and --to are optional and parsed into an inclusive-exclusive window applied after the value is composed. There is no fail-closed on a whole-person read. The enforcement is the absence of a required parameter in TimelineQuery and the bounds parser treating an empty bound as open.

W is the anam-event sequence itself: one number, gapless, never stored The watermark is MAX(seq); a trigger lets an event take only the next seq; every event kind advances it, a pure status transition included.

invariant supporting basis: code internal/store/ledger/ledger.sql#anam_event_seq_follows anchor WHEN NEW.seq IS NOT (SELECT COALESCE(MAX(seq), 0) + 1 FROM anam_event)

ARCHITECTURE §7. Because the five kinds are all the anam-events there are and every one can change the live-set, nothing takes a seq without moving the watermark and nothing moves the watermark without taking a seq. The ledger stores no counter. A batch takes a contiguous block, stamped with one anam-time, so anam-time is non-decreasing and ties are broken by seq. A watermark means nothing against another ledger, which is why receipts and the index pin the ledger id.

What Anam authors is authored, never authoritative Governing metadata, the ledger order, watermarks, checkpoints, receipts and provisional derived findings are Anam's own operational record: backed up, never rebuilt from a source, never the truth about the world.

invariant supporting basis: docs docs/ARCHITECTURE.md#Invariants anchor is **authored, never authoritative**; backed up, not rebuilt.

Applies to Ledger store (internal/store/ledger), Operational store (internal/store/ops), Operational store

Enforced at not enforced

ARCHITECTURE §12, second bullet. The sources are the truth about the world; the ledger is the truth about what Anam knew. The operational store is a real store, authoritative for what Anam authors, which is why each of its tables says where it comes back from: the derived tier by replay, the provisioned tier from the binary and the importers, the authored tier from a backup and nowhere else.

An earlier draft said SQLite holds nothing that cannot be rebuilt by replaying the ledger; that demoted a real database to a cache and was corrected. Nothing enforces the tier annotations; they are comments in the schema, and the snapshot tables add a fourth word, disposable.

A batch is at most 49 envelopes, admitted whole or not at all The gate's batch is one transaction and one anam-time instant; the daemon refuses more than 49 with 400 and the importer base cuts at 49.

docs/IMPORTERS.md §8.4. The number is inherited from the previous substrate's transaction limit and kept so that one batch is one change in Anam's knowledge. The ledger sets no limit of its own; the size is the gate's policy. All-or-nothing follows from the transaction: any refusal rolls the whole batch back.

docs/IMPORTERS.md §8.3, as in a schema registry. "Same body" compares canonical JSON, so reformatting a file is harmless; a change is a new version. The importer side checks the same thing with dict equality before posting and stops the run on a disagreement.

Agent findings are derived facets admitted as proposed; endorsement never writes back An insight re-enters through the same gate as a proposed derived facet carrying its method and the watermark it was computed from; endorsed and rejected are anam-events that never reach a source.

Every external call carries a deadline and fails fast Every store method takes a context with a deadline; every daemon write runs under its job's deadline; a missing ledger is an error on open, never created by reading.

invariant detail basis: code internal/daemon/queue.go#run anchor ctx, cancel := context.WithDeadline(base, until)

ARCHITECTURE §12, last bullet, second half, and AGENTS.md. The daemon takes the shorter of its own timeout and the caller's Anam-Timeout header, and the wait for the write queue spends that budget (card 110, see queue wait spends the caller's budget); the ledger's busy timeout (3 s) sits below the default call timeout (5 s) so contention surfaces as ErrBusy, not a bare deadline; the importer's client bounds the whole call with one deadline and caps the answer. On the importer side, a source that fails names whether a retry can help.

ARCHITECTURE §7. A width that defaulted quietly would be the worst kind of wrong: the ids it wrote would be well-formed, plausible, and afterwards indistinguishable from correct ones. So a typo dies at config load, not on the ingest path, and facet_index.bucket_id is a property of the built index, not of the run. Change paths (5)

Add a source as an importer A new vendor source is a folder in anam-importers with a manifest, a run.py that shapes payloads, and the Definitions it emits, plus a token line in the daemon's importers file.

change-path supporting basis: docs ../anam-importers/README.md#Writing an importer anchor ## Writing an importer

Touches Importer base (anam_importer), anam_submit, Definition manifest, Importers file, Daemon, Admit a batch, Register a Definition, A type lives in one place, An importer shapes payloads and nothing else

  1. Decide the type. If Anam bundles a Definition for it (28 under internal/def/manifests), the importer carries no Definition for that type and shapes to the current version; several importers may share it. Otherwise write <folder>/definitions/<type>.json in the manifest format, Open mHealth where a vocabulary exists, and no other importer may name it.
  2. Write the folder. manifest.yaml (protocol 2, commands handle, poll, health, the same version as VERSION), run.py with a uv script header for jsonschema. run.py reads the source and builds payloads only: envelope, check, batches, one <plugin>.batch event per group, and snapshot_parts on a resend-everything run. Raise Failure(message, retry=...) when the source fails.
  3. Tests. Unit tests beside the others under tests/, and an ANAM_BIN test against a real daemon that admits once and resends as no-op. test_version.py fails if the manifest's version disagrees.
  4. Token and scope. openssl rand -hex 32; its SHA-256 goes in the daemon's importers file with the source name and types, and the daemon restarts to read it. The token goes in the gateway vault under its own name, granted to the anam_submit_<source> principal.
  5. Gateway. An anam_submit instance with token_secret, a trigger (webhook, folder watch, schedule), and pipelines from the trigger to the importer and from <plugin>.batch to the instance. Route anam.snapshot_absent to a notifier.
  6. Cut-over, if it takes over an existing source. Use that source's name so facets are the same facets, as anam_records and anam_documents did in card 119; the first resend should be all no-op. Replacing a source under a new name instead ends with anam retract <old> --retired.
Change a Definition's shape with a new version A new version may change the schema, classification or series but never identity_key or fact_time; bundled types change in the binary, registered ones in the importer's folder.

change-path supporting basis: code internal/def/guard.go#Guard anchor func Guard(stored []Stored, bundled *Registry) error {

Touches Definition manifest, Definitions registry (internal/def), Definitions live in the operational store, not the ledger, source_ref is identity; content_hash is content, Admission gate, Register a Definition, anam_submit

  1. **Bump definition_version** in the manifest. The gate refuses an envelope shaped to any other version, so every importer of the type must move with it; an importer that still carries the old version will have its batches refused with ErrDefinitionVersion.
  2. Keep the keying. identity_key, identity_template and time_semantics.fact_time must be identical; def.Guard refuses the binary at startup and def.Register refuses an importer with ErrKeyed. A new placing or keying rule is a new ledger from the sources. Two widenings pass (card 198): adding a zone_pointer to the fact time, and appending optional placeholders ({/pointer?}) to the template, each only while no stored version of the type declared the field it reads.
  3. Bundled type. Edit internal/def/manifests/<type>.json; the embedded set and def.SetVersion change with the build. Facets admitted under the old version stay interpretable because every version is held in the definition table once an ingest has run the guard. Add the version's semantics to the type's test under internal/def.
  4. Registered type. Edit <importer>/definitions/<type>.json; the next batch GETs the new version, gets a 404, and registers it (201). A different body at an already held version is a 409 and stops the importer.
  5. Existing facets are not re-shaped. Admission is idempotent on content, so a Definition that changes its series key reaches only facets admitted after the change; re-send the source if the old rows must carry the new shape, which supersedes them.
  6. Records. A records file names no version; anam_records stamps the current one, so regenerate the file from its extractor with the same ids and resend.
Add a read command that works locally and over the daemon A new read lives in internal/read against a read.Source, gets a daemon route on the read-only handles, a client method, and a cobra command that routes on cfg.Server.

change-path detail basis: code internal/app/timeline.go#readTimeline anchor if cfg.Server != "" {

Touches CLI (internal/app), Client (internal/client), Daemon, Read (internal/read), Each route names the roles it admits, Reads over the daemon are bounded by bytes, Commands that only read open the ledger read-only, Read the timeline

  1. Wire types in internal/api: a query with Values() and an Of(url.Values) parser, and a result struct. Both sides share them, so names cannot drift.
  2. The read in internal/read, taking read.Source (ledger, checkpoints, artifacts, Definitions). Compose from checkpoints through mint.ReadAt or ReadWindow where it can; fall back to the fold. Keep payloads unparsed.
  3. The route in internal/daemon/api.go on the read-only handles (d.read, d.readOps), as an entry in the route table that names the roles it admits (anyRole for a read; see each route names the roles it admits), with d.timeout(r) as the deadline and read.Fit against max_read_bytes before writing. No turn on the write queue.
  4. The client method in internal/client, one per route, and a command in internal/app that calls loadRoutedConfig and branches on cfg.Server: client when set, otherwise readSource with the ledger opened read-only.
  5. Tests. A test in internal/app that runs the command both ways against a real ledger and ops file in t.TempDir() and asserts the same answer and the same refusals, as publish_internal_test.go does.
  6. check.sh must stay green: no SQL outside the store, gofmt, vet, lint, gosec, race.
Add or change a projection template A template is one embedded JSON file; a changed prune is a new version, because receipts pin the version and verify refuses a moved one.

change-path detail basis: code internal/projection/manifest.go#Bundled anchor //go:embed manifests/*.json

Touches Projection template, Projection, Serve (internal/serve), Receipt, Projections prune, never reshape or generate; a consumer selects, never defines, Publish a projection

  1. Add internal/projection/manifests/<name>.json with name, version, types, statuses, max_classification, from, to and optionally shape: snapshot. The file name must equal name.
  2. A snapshot shape needs the types to share a series family in their Definitions, or the shape has nothing to group on.
  3. Changing an existing template's filters bumps version. Receipts served under the old version still verify only if the old pruning can be reproduced, which it cannot once the file changed: Verify reports ErrTemplateMoved. Treat a template as immutable per version.
  4. anam serve with no argument lists it; anam timeline --projection <name> --explain shows each facet's verdict; the inspector's /projection switches between templates.
  5. The explain tests run every template over random values; add the template's name to the table in explain_test.go if a test enumerates them.
Change the bucket width or the checkpoint budget The budget can change at any time and the next mint settles it; the width is pinned into the index, so changing it is a rebuild of tracking.db and a full mint.

change-path detail basis: code internal/store/ops/ops.go#pinBucketWidth anchor stored, fresh, err := pin(ctx, db, "bucket_width", string(w))

Touches Config keys, Buckets (internal/span), Mint, Operational store (internal/store/ops), The bucket width is checked once at config load and pinned into the index, Bucket on fact-time, monthly by default, subdivided by a byte budget, A weekly bucket width disables composed reads and incremental mints

Budget (checkpoint_budget, bytes). Lower it and the next mint finds every checkpoint file over the new budget by its size and splits those buckets along the calendar; raise it and nothing merges, because splits only add. Set it to 0 to keep the fixed bucket_width with no subdivision, which also makes every mint a full one.

Width (bucket_width, Monthly or Weekly). The operational store refuses a file built at another width. The order: stop the daemon, change the config, delete tracking.db, run anam init to rebuild it, start the daemon (its first write replays the ledger into the index), then anam checkpoint --full. Facets and events are untouched; the checkpoint values under the old ids go with the deleted tracking.db, and the rebuilt one holds only the values the new mint writes.

Weekly is accepted but does not subdivide a year, so the composed read and the incremental mint refuse it and fold in full; see the gap before choosing it.

Gaps (17)

A mint that fails after an importer batch is reported only in the daemon log mintOnRequest discards the result of mintAtHead, so a failed background mint reaches neither the batch's response nor GET /v1/status; only an operator mint over POST /v1/checkpoints returns its error.

gap

Looked at internal/daemon/minting.go; internal/daemon/envelopes.go; internal/daemon/api.go

mintAtHead returns an api.MintRun with Error and logs the failure; mintOnRequest calls it as a statement. Until card 119 a declared ingest kept that result on its operation as mint.error; with POST /v1/ingest gone nothing keeps it. The next changing batch or a restart (mintIfBehind) retries, and reads fold in full meanwhile, so nothing is wrong, only unseen. See mint cadence follows the ledger.

  • mentioned by Mint cadence follows the ledger
A reader is not narrowed to projections The reader role opens every read route, so a reader sees the whole record; docs/API.md calls narrowing a reader to some projections later work, and no code does it.

gap

Looked at docs/API.md; internal/daemon/api.go; internal/config/principals.go

routes() marks GET /v1/timeline, GET /v1/changes and the other reads anyRole, and a Principal has no field that restricts which projection or types it may read; an importer's Types bound what it submits, not what it reads. API.md §2 says the roles are the floor and a reader narrowed to some projections is later work, not a role. Until then a reader token is as wide as the operator's on reads, minus the write routes and the inspector. A query may carry its projection inline (card 211), which widens nothing today; if readers are ever narrowed to some projections, an inline one must sit under the reader's ceiling. See principals and roles.

A weekly bucket width disables composed reads and incremental mints The docs say weekly is also implemented; span.New accepts it, but a weekly id does not subdivide a year, so LayoutOf refuses it and every read and mint falls back to the full fold.

gap

Looked at internal/span/layout.go; internal/mint/read.go; internal/mint/incremental.go; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md; docs/GLOSSARY.md

Layout subdivides years into months into days; LayoutOf returns an error for a week id. startingRun and fromLastRun then report ok=false, and partition with a budget fails with a message to clear the log and mint again. A weekly configuration therefore works only with checkpoint_budget: 0, and then every timeline read folds the whole ledger. ARCHITECTURE §7 says checkpoint_budget: 0 keeps the fixed width but not that weekly loses the composed path. Whether weekly is meant to survive the subdivision design is not recorded. See buckets.

Sentences in ARCHITECTURE.md the code has moved past Zero-padded sort keys, RFC3339Nano times, retract reads the full fold, commit returns admitted or rejected, and a ledger busy lock as a 503; each is one sentence behind a later card.

2026-10-22, card 200

  • Code: anam migrate carries the live facets of one ledger into a fresh one. The old ledger and operational store are opened read-only; the new pair must not exist. The selection is every live facet at its head, folded by the one fold, or those a program's pick/1 selects over the canonical. Each is admitted through the gate by CarryBatch, held to the Definition version it was admitted under (Holding, def.Versions), so the id, hash and fact time are the old ones; the registered Definitions and projections are copied first (ops.ReadRegistered, CarryRegistered). A dry run reports per type and source and writes nothing; a refusal anywhere stops a real run. A record.edge end the selection does not carry is listed. After --yes the command proves the new ledger's live facets equal the selection and that a dry run against it selects the same set. catchup.EdgeEnds is exported for the links. long_write_timeout also bounds each phase.
  • Bundle: the workflow migrate-to-a-fresh-ledger, a migrate row on the CLI page, the gate and store-ops pages.

2026-10-22, card 200 (current version)

The dry run prints MOVED and OWN per type and source and a line per group moved or kept; the proof reports the moves apart from the identity check. Matt's decision of 2026-10-22.

2026-10-22, card 215

  • Code: checkpoint values, receipts, served values and saved selections are rows of the operational store, not files under artifact_dir. checkpoint_value is derived and refuses an update; receipt, served_value, saved_selection and saved_selection_code are authored and immutable by trigger. The schema is 9, so a version-8 store is refused and carried forward by rebuilding it and running anam move-artifacts --from <folder> [--registered-from <old tracking.db>] --yes, which hashes every file against its name before it writes and loads the checkpoint values the log names. Matt's decision: a checkpoint is a cache that is allowed to go missing, and a missing one means fold past it and mint it again. mint_run gains picks, a digest of the checkpoints a read from the run takes, and mint_seal is gone; RunCheckpoints takes them in one transaction and says when the log no longer yields the ones the run saw, and then ReadAt and ReadWindow decline and the caller folds in full, as they do for a value that is not in the store. A mint writes back a value the log names and the store lost, and ends by deleting the checkpoint rows, sizes and values the newest run does not read (CollectCheckpoints). internal/store/artifact and the config key artifact_dir are gone; a backup is the ledger, the operational store and the library.
  • Bundle: the artifact store component and the atomic-writes decision are replaced by the stored-content decision; the checkpoint value, checkpoint log, store-ops, config keys, mint and read workflows follow.

2026-10-22, card 216

  • Code: a receipt can name its reader. receipt.Receipt.Principal is last and omitted when empty, so every receipt cut before it keeps its bytes and hash and still verifies (TestPrincipal_OldReceiptKeepsItsBytesAndHash). A principal in the importers file may set receipts: always (reader or importer only; validated at load). The daemon then files that token's GET /v1/timeline as POST /v1/receipts files a read, with status 200, naming the principal; POST /v1/query names it on the receipt it already files, and dry_run still files nothing. Such a token must name a projection and cannot use explain (400), opens only the receipts that name it (403), and its read fails if the receipt cannot be filed. serve.Deps.Reader carries the name; serve.List takes a ListFilter, GET /v1/receipts takes principal, since, until and zone, and anam receipt list takes --principal, --since and --until and shows a reader column.

2026-10-22, card 211

2026-10-22, the operator admission hold

  • Code: an operator can hold a source's admission at the daemon's door. POST /v1/hold (operator) takes {"sources":[...]} or {"all":true}, DELETE /v1/hold releases, and GET /v1/status reports the held sources in held. A held source's POST /v1/envelopes and POST /v1/definitions are refused with 503 and a Retry-After, and the body names the hold; the check is inside the write, so a hold and an admission are one order. Reads, the operator's own writes and POST /v1/entry (the workbench) are never held. The hold is authored state in the operational store (admission_hold, schema version 9), so it survives a restart of the daemon and is carried into a new store by anam migrate. anam hold <source>..., anam hold --all, anam hold --release [<source>...] and anam hold to list.
  • Bundle: the daemon component's route table, the CLI component's command table, the store-ops component's table and the operational-store term.
